===== FILE: AperaOwnSocket.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="AperaOwnSocket" access="private" >
    <Parameters xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Param/1" >
      <Parameter name="x_bFree" type="bool" use="reference" xsi:type="array" dimensions="1" />
    </Parameters>
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_nHB" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nT0" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  Blocks until the single unlocked APERA socket is PROVABLY free, then
  //  returns x_bFree=true.
  //
  //  *** IF THIS RETURNS FALSE THE CALLER MUST MAKE NO apera_* CALL. ***
  //  Not a trigger, not a wait, not an inspect, not clearBuffer. False means
  //  tkBeltScan stopped advancing its heartbeat, which most likely means it is
  //  stopped INSIDE apera_send - between its write to sAperaConn and its read
  //  of the reply. Writing that socket then makes one task read the other's
  //  reply, and apera_wait_for_trajectory pulls binary waypoints into
  //  jAperaTrajectory which apera_follow_trajectory replays into movej().
  //  That is an arm-crash, so on false the caller stops the belt and ends the
  //  cycle instead. A stall the operator can see beats a wrong trajectory.
  //
  //  Exists as one program, called from every site that needs the socket, so
  //  there is exactly ONE copy of this logic to get right. Three earlier
  //  revisions of mine each hand-rolled a variant of it and each got a
  //  different part wrong.
  //
  //  WHAT "FREE" MEANS - all three, and each is load-bearing:
  //    bScanDone==true       the acknowledgement. tkBeltScan writes this LAST,
  //                          after it is out of apera_send.
  //    bScanForParts==false  not handed out. Tested as well as bScanDone
  //                          because tkCheckZone claims the socket by writing
  //                          bScanDone=false then bScanForParts=true, and
  //                          tkBeltScan's idle branch may set bScanDone=true
  //                          in between - so bScanDone alone can read "free"
  //                          during a claim that is still committing.
  //    no kick pending       bBeltLiftArmed with the kick enabled on the last
  //                          part means tkCheckZone may claim the socket at
  //                          any instant, including between this test and the
  //                          caller's apera call. Self-limiting: tkCheckZone
  //                          clears the arm within nBeltKickTimeout_s either
  //                          way.
  //
  //  THE WATCHDOG BOUNDS SILENCE, NOT TOTAL WAIT. A long wait here is often
  //  correct - it is the cell parked out of shot while the belt hunts for
  //  parts, and it recovers on its own when parts arrive. So the clock is
  //  restarted whenever nScanHeartbeat moves AND a scan is genuinely in
  //  progress (bScanForParts==true and bScanDone==false). Both halves of that
  //  qualifier matter: tkBeltScan advances the heartbeat in its IDLE branch as
  //  well, so resetting on the heartbeat alone - or on bScanForParts alone -
  //  would disarm the watchdog entirely in the handed-out-but-idle state, and
  //  a pending kick that never resolves (tkCheckZone dead) would hang forever.
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude)
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  l_nT0=clock()
  l_nHB=nScanHeartbeat
  x_bFree=false
  //  do..until, not while: the test MUST run at least once. nScanTimeout_s is
  //  public, and with a leading while a value of 0 would skip the test
  //  entirely and report the socket busy on every single call - stopping the
  //  belt and ending the cycle forever. This shape is correct for any value.
  //  When the socket is already free the body runs once, takes no delay(), and
  //  exits - so on the ordinary path (kick disabled) this costs nothing.
  do
    //  End Cycle / Stop: ask the scan to stand down so the operator's press is
    //  honoured here rather than ignored until the wait ends. tkBeltScan exits
    //  within one detect round-trip and acknowledges via bScanDone.
    if bEndCycleRobot==true or bStart==false
      bScanCancel=true
    endIf
    //  Reset the silence clock only while a scan is ACTUALLY RUNNING.
    //  bScanForParts alone is not that test: tkBeltScan advances the heartbeat
    //  in its idle branch too, so resetting on bScanForParts&&heartbeat would
    //  disarm the watchdog in the handed-out-but-idle state. bScanDone==false
    //  is what distinguishes "in the scan branch" from "idle".
    if bScanForParts==true and bScanDone==false and nScanHeartbeat!=l_nHB
      l_nHB=nScanHeartbeat
      l_nT0=clock()
    endIf
    x_bFree=(bScanDone==true and bScanForParts==false)
    if bEarlyBeltKick==true and bLastPart==true and bBeltLiftArmed==true
      x_bFree=false
    endIf
    if x_bFree==false
      delay(0.05)
    endIf
  until (x_bFree==true or (clock()-l_nT0)>=nScanTimeout_s)
  if x_bFree==false
    //  Drop the belt - whatever state the scan is in, it is not being
    //  supervised any more - and leave the request standing.
    bScanCancel=true
    bOUTPUTS["Output3"]=false
    logMsg("RAIV/T1: APERA socket not released in "+toString("",nScanTimeout_s)+"s of silence - caller must make no apera call",1)
  endIf
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: AperaPartsFound.pgx =====
