===== FILE: TryCaptureNow.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="TryCaptureNow" access="private" >
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //  v2.3.0 (Oct 2026, Cowork/Claude). NON-BLOCKING trajectory capture.
  //  Takes the trajectory picture NOW if, and only if, every condition holds:
  //    no picture taken yet for this pick (bTrajTriggered false), cell
  //    running, no pick in flight, belt stopped AND settled with a plate on
  //    the beam (bBeltReadyForCapture - see tkBeltSensor), arm out of the
  //    APERA view (_bStartFoV false), APERA socket not held by the belt scan.
  //  Otherwise it does nothing and returns at once. It never moves the arm
  //  and never waits, so it is safe to call from inside a motion poll loop
  //  (WaitMoveCapture). Nested ifs, no reliance on short-circuit "and".
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  if bTrajTriggered==false and bStart==true and bEndCycleRobot==false and bAbort==false and bPickInFlight==false
    if bBeltReadyForCapture==true and _bStartFoV==false and bScanDone==true and bScanForParts==false
      nTrajBeltStarts=nBeltMasterStarts
      bTrajBeltOk=bBeltReadyForCapture
      call apera:apera_trigger_trajectory(sPipeline,jTrajectoryStart,jTrajectoryEnd)
      _nTrigClk=clock()
      bTrajTriggered=true
      nCapInMotion=nCapInMotion+1
    endIf
  endIf
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>


===== FILE: UpdateCycleWindow.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="UpdateCycleWindow" access="public" >
    <Parameters xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Param/1" >
      <Parameter name="x_nBuffer" type="num" use="reference" xsi:type="array" dimensions="1" />
      <Parameter name="x_nIndex" type="num" use="reference" xsi:type="element" dimensions="1" />
      <Parameter name="x_nNewSample" type="num" use="value" xsi:type="element" dimensions="1" />
      <Parameter name="x_sPrefix" type="string" use="value" xsi:type="element" dimensions="1" />
      <Parameter name="x_bComputePPM" type="bool" use="value" xsi:type="element" dimensions="1" />
    </Parameters>
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_nBest" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nCount" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nI" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nSize" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nSum" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nWorse" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  Generic rolling-buffer helper shared by every cycle-time and per-
  //  segment timer on Raiv-T1. Called once per completed sample:
  //    1) Pushes x_nNewSample into x_nBuffer at x_nIndex (ring buffer,
  //       wraps using size(x_nBuffer), so the SAME function works for the
  //       10/25/50/100/1000-sample cycle-time windows and every per-
  //       segment buffer just by passing a different array).
  //    2) Rescans the whole buffer to (re)compute Last / Average / Best /
  //       Worse and a fill count, ignoring slots that are still 0 (not yet
  //       recorded) - same convention the original cycle-time code used.
  //    3) Writes the results into the existing _sCycleTime[] collection
  //       using keys built as x_sPrefix+"AverageSec", x_sPrefix+"BestPPM",
  //       etc. x_sPrefix="" reproduces the original, unprefixed keys
  //       (LastSec/AverageSec/BestSec/WorseSec/...) so the existing
  //       Production page bindings keep working unchanged.
  //  x_bComputePPM should be false for per-segment timers - parts-per-
  //  minute has no meaning for a sub-segment of the cycle, only for the
  //  whole cycle-time windows.
  //
  //  Every new key referenced here (see the list below) must already
  //  exist in the _sCycleTime collection in binpick_TiePlates.dtx - VAL3
  //  collections cannot be extended with new keys at runtime.
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : Aug 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - added to support the 25/50/100/
  //             1000-sample rolling cycle-time windows and per-segment
  //             timing shown on the new "Cycle Timing" SP2 page.
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  l_nSize=size(x_nBuffer)
  //
  //Push the new sample into the ring buffer
  x_nBuffer[x_nIndex]=x_nNewSample
  x_nIndex=(x_nIndex+1)%l_nSize
  //
  //Rescan the buffer for Average / Best / Worse, ignoring empty (0) slots
  l_nSum=0
  l_nCount=0
  l_nBest=power(2,100)
  l_nWorse=0
  for l_nI=0 to l_nSize-1
    if x_nBuffer[l_nI]!=0
      l_nSum=l_nSum+x_nBuffer[l_nI]
      l_nCount=l_nCount+1
      if x_nBuffer[l_nI]<l_nBest
        l_nBest=x_nBuffer[l_nI]
      endIf
      if x_nBuffer[l_nI]>l_nWorse
        l_nWorse=x_nBuffer[l_nI]
      endIf
    endIf
  endFor
  //
  //Last sample (guard against a bogus 0-second sample, same as original code)
  if x_nNewSample!=0
    _sCycleTime[x_sPrefix+"LastSec"]=toString("3.2",x_nNewSample)
    if x_bComputePPM==true
      _sCycleTime[x_sPrefix+"LastPPM"]=toString("3.0",60/x_nNewSample)
    endIf
  endIf
  //
  //Fill indicator, e.g. "12/25" - lets the HMI show a window is still filling
  _sCycleTime[x_sPrefix+"CountOf"]=toString("4.0",l_nCount)+"/"+toString("4.0",l_nSize)
  //
  if l_nCount!=0
    _sCycleTime[x_sPrefix+"AverageSec"]=toString("3.2",l_nSum/l_nCount)
    _sCycleTime[x_sPrefix+"BestSec"]=toString("3.2",l_nBest)
    _sCycleTime[x_sPrefix+"WorseSec"]=toString("3.2",l_nWorse)
    if x_bComputePPM==true
      _sCycleTime[x_sPrefix+"AveragePPM"]=toString("3.0",60/(l_nSum/l_nCount))
      _sCycleTime[x_sPrefix+"BestPPM"]=toString("3.0",60/l_nBest)
      _sCycleTime[x_sPrefix+"WorsePPM"]=toString("3.0",60/l_nWorse)
    endIf
  endIf
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: WaitBeltParts.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="WaitBeltParts" access="private" >
    <Parameters xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Param/1" >
      <Parameter name="x_bReady" type="bool" use="reference" xsi:type="array" dimensions="1" />
    </Parameters>
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_bLogged" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nT0" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  Blocks until the conveyor has delivered a plate to the pick end and
  //  stopped, or until the cycle stands down. This is the ONLY way the rest
  //  of the application is allowed to ask "is there something to pick" now
  //  that the belt belongs to tkBeltSensor.
  //
  //  Returns x_bReady:
  //    true   a plate is on the beam, the belt is stopped and settled, and
  //           nothing is in alarm. It is safe to capture - AFTER the caller
  //           has separately satisfied itself that the arm is out of the
  //           conveyor camera's view.
  //    false  the cycle is standing down (Stop / End Cycle / Abort), the belt
  //           master is in alarm, or nBeltWaitTimeout_s elapsed.
  //
  //  ***  IT DOES NOT TOUCH THE BELT.  ***
  //  There is exactly one writer of bOUTPUTS["Output3"] in this application
  //  and it is tkBeltSensor. This program waits; it never asks for the belt,
  //  never nudges it and never stops it. If you find yourself wanting to add
  //  a belt write here, the flag to publish is nNoPickOnBeam - tkBeltSensor
  //  already watches it and owns the nudge.
  //
  //  ***  WHY THE TIMEOUT IS NOT A FAILURE  ***
  //  nBeltWaitTimeout_s (ships 60) exists so the caller gets control back and
  //  can re-test End Cycle, not because 60 s of waiting is wrong. An empty
  //  feeder legitimately means a long wait, and the belt is running for all
  //  of it. The caller is expected to loop. tkBeltSensor's own runaway cap
  //  (nBeltRunMax_s) is what bounds the belt itself.
  //
  //  ***  LIVENESS  ***
  //  A public global holds its last value for ever if the task that writes it
  //  dies, so a dead tkBeltSensor would leave bBeltReadyForCapture frozen -
  //  frozen true would send the cell picking at a stopped belt for ever,
  //  frozen false would hang here. nBeltSensorHeartbeat is therefore checked
  //  for movement, and a dead belt master is reported and treated as "not
  //  ready" rather than trusted.
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - sensor-only belt control.
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  x_bReady=false
  l_bLogged=false
  l_nT0=clock()
  nBeltWaitCalls=nBeltWaitCalls+1
  //  Heartbeat sample for the liveness test below.
  _nBeltHB0=nBeltSensorHeartbeat
  _nBeltHBClk=clock()
  while x_bReady==false and bStart==true and bEndCycleRobot==false and bAbort==false and (clock()-l_nT0)<nBeltWaitTimeout_s
    //--- is the belt master alive -----------------------------------------
    if nBeltSensorHeartbeat!=_nBeltHB0
      _nBeltHB0=nBeltSensorHeartbeat
      _nBeltHBClk=clock()
    elseIf (clock()-_nBeltHBClk)>=nBeltSensorTimeout_s
      if l_bLogged==false
        l_bLogged=true
        nBeltMasterDead=nBeltMasterDead+1
        logMsg("RAIV/T1: belt master heartbeat is NOT MOVING - tkBeltSensor is dead or was never created. The conveyor is uncontrolled. Re-run monitoring() and check the .pjx.",3)
      endIf
    endIf
    //--- the alarm is an exit, not something to wait out -------------------
    if bBeamBlockedAlarm==true
      nBeltWaitAlarms=nBeltWaitAlarms+1
      return
    endIf
    if bBeltReadyForCapture==true
      x_bReady=true
    else
      delay(nBeltSensorPoll_s)
    endIf
  endWhile
  nBeltWait_s=clock()-l_nT0
  if x_bReady==false and bStart==true and bEndCycleRobot==false and bAbort==false and bBeamBlockedAlarm==false
    nBeltWaitTimeouts=nBeltWaitTimeouts+1
    logMsg("RAIV/T1: waited "+toString("",nBeltWaitTimeout_s)+"s for a plate at the belt sensor and none arrived - conveyor is still feeding, retrying",1)
  endIf
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: WaitFovClear.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="WaitFovClear" access="private" >
    <Parameters xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Param/1" >
      <Parameter name="x_bClear" type="bool" use="reference" xsi:type="array" dimensions="1" />
    </Parameters>
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_nT0" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  Waits until the arm is out of the conveyor camera's field of view, so
  //  that the picture taken next images the BELT and not the gripper.
  //  Returns x_bClear true if it got there, false if it timed out.
  //
  //  ***  WHY THIS EXISTS AS ITS OWN PROGRAM  ***
  //  "Take the picture with the robot out of the camera FoV" is now a
  //  requirement of every capture in the cycle, not a per-site option, so it
  //  is written once and counted once. Every caller that captures calls this
  //  first.
  //
  //  ***  WHAT _bStartFoV ACTUALLY MEANS  ***
  //  tkCheckZone builds two world-axis-aligned cubes and its own original
  //  comments name them correctly: cube 1 is "APERA IN Field of View", cube 2
  //  is "APERA OUT Field of View". They meet at one boundary. _bStartFoV true
  //  means the gripper is IN the camera's view of the conveyor.
  //  The boundary is nStartFoVX_mm from pStartCnvyrFoV. It ships 1075, which
  //  puts the face at world x = +550 - the position measured at the cell in
  //  Sep 2026 as the point where the gripper AND the plate it is holding have
  //  fully left the camera. Before that it was +310, and for those 240 mm the
  //  flag read "clear" while the camera could still see the held plate, which
  //  is how a plate on the magnet came to be counted as a plate on the belt
  //  on 180 of 180 recorded frames.
  //
  //  ***  THE TIMEOUT IS A REPORT, NOT A GATE  ***
  //  If the arm cannot clear the cube - because it is parked inside it, or
  //  because nStartFoVX_mm has been set to a boundary the cycle's poses never
  //  cross - this returns false and nFovDeclines counts it. The CALLER
  //  decides what to do. It must not simply capture anyway: on 15 Sep an
  //  earlier version of this waited the full nFovClearTimeout_s on every
  //  single pick, because the capture pose sat 12 mm inside the new boundary.
  //  A non-zero nFovDeclines with a flat cycle time means exactly that, and
  //  the fix is the capture POSE, not the timeout.
  //  Read nFovDeclines on the first run. It should be 0.
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - sensor-only belt control.
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  x_bClear=false
  l_nT0=clock()
  while _bStartFoV==true and (clock()-l_nT0)<nFovClearTimeout_s and bStart==true and bEndCycleRobot==false and bAbort==false
    delay(0.02)
  endWhile
  nFovWait_s=clock()-l_nT0
  if _bStartFoV==false
    x_bClear=true
    nCapFovClear=nCapFovClear+1
  else
    nFovDeclines=nFovDeclines+1
    if bStart==true and bEndCycleRobot==false and bAbort==false
      logMsg("RAIV/T1: arm did not leave the APERA view in "+toString("",nFovClearTimeout_s)+"s - capture pose is inside the cube. Check nStartFoVX_mm and jApera_Trigger_pos.",2)
    endIf
  endIf
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: WaitMoveCapture.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="WaitMoveCapture" access="private" >
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //  v2.3.0 (Oct 2026, Cowork/Claude). Polls TryCaptureNow() every 10 ms
  //  until the motion queue is empty, then once more. Use it IN PLACE OF a
  //  bare wait for motion: the trajectory picture is taken the instant the
  //  belt has settled and the arm is out of the camera view, wherever the arm
  //  is in the place/return, so APERA works while the arm is still moving.
  //  It does NOT stop the arm - follow it with waitEndMove() only where a
  //  full stop is actually required.
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  while isEmpty()==false
    call TryCaptureNow()
    delay(0.01)
  endWhile
  call TryCaptureNow()
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>


===== FILE: WarmUp.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="WarmUp" access="private" >
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_pHere" type="pointRx" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  //  This Routine will loop Robot arm through motion extreams to warm up Robot joint in cold weather startup.
  //  Minimun operatinig temperature provided from Staubli is 5-degrees on Motor Probe and DSI 
  //  This routine will be accessed from the Monitoring page where temperature values are also visable..
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  //  Created 2/21/2025 Coty Coots
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  //  Set Logic bWarmUp to indicate this routine is running
  bWarmUp=true
  //
  //  Routine started from Stowed Position
  l_pHere=here(tAR70,world)
  if bRobotAtStow==true
    trAppro.z=-25
    movel(appro(l_pHere,trAppro),tAR70,mSlow)
    movej(jHome,tAR70,mSlow)
  endIf
  //  Robot in Home Position
  //  Loop Points until 
  //  -----------------------[Commanded to Stop]----???????????? 
  //  bStow will end the Warm Up routine and Stow Robot
  //
  while (bStow==false)
    movej(jWarmUp[0],tAR70,mCycleSlow)
    movej(jWarmUp[1],tAR70,mCycleSlow)
    movej(jWarmUp[2],tAR70,mCycleSlow)
    movej(jWarmUp[3],tAR70,mCycleSlow)
    movej(jWarmUp[1],tAR70,mCycleSlow)
    movej(jWarmUp[4],tAR70,mCycleSlow)
    movej(jWarmUp[5],tAR70,mCycleSlow)
    movej(jWarmUp[4],tAR70,mCycleSlow)
    movej(jWarmUp[0],tAR70,mCycleSlow)
    wait(isEmpty())
  endWhile
  //
  //Once Commanded to Stop WarmUp; Finish loop, Move to home, to Stow, Initilize variables
  wait(isEmpty())
  movej(jHome,tAR70,mSlow)
  waitEndMove()
  wait(bRobotAtHome==true)
  link(pStow,world)
  pStow=jointToPoint(tAR70,world,jStow)
  movej(appro(pStow,{0,0,-50,0,0,0}),tAR70,mSlow)
  movel(pStow,tAR70,mSlow)
  waitEndMove()
  call init()

end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: clickAbort.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="clickAbort" access="private" >
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_pHere" type="pointRx" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_pStow" type="pointRx" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  Abort cycle, stop motion and kill "cycle" task
  // 
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : July 2024 A. Cisneros (Stäubli)
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  l_pStow=jointToPoint(tAR70,world,jStow)
  stopMove()
  resetMotion()
  dioSet(dDOConveyorON,0)
  //  Turn off gripperON signal if ABORT selected during "call gripper ON" routine. CTC 4/21
  dioSet(dDOemh_gripperON,0)
  dioSet(dDOAirStream,0)
  bOUTPUTS["Output3"]=false
  //  Early belt advance: drop the arm so an abort taken between the gripper
  //  closing and the part lifting cannot leave a kick pending for later.
  bBeltLiftArmed=false
  bLastPart=false
  bEarlyKickFired=false
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnStart","disabled",true)
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnPause","disabled",true)
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnEndCycle","disabled",true)
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnStart","style.backgroundColor","#B6B6B6")
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnAbort","style.backgroundColor","#F85757")
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnAbort","disabled",true)
  l_pHere=here(tAR70,world)
  //Check Robot position and find better path to move safely to jHome
  //Robot in StartFoV area
  if l_pHere.trsf.z>30 and l_pHere.trsf.z<180 and _bStartFoV==true
    restartMove()
    movej(jHome,tAR70,mSlow)
    waitEndMove()
  elseIf l_pHere.trsf.z<30 and _bStartFoV==true
    trAppro.z=-150
    restartMove()
    movel(appro(l_pHere,trAppro),tAR70,mSlow)
    movej(jHome,tAR70,mSlow)
    waitEndMove()
    //
    //Robot in EndFoV area
  elseIf _bEndFoV==true and l_pHere.trsf.z<-130
    trAppro.z=-80
    restartMove()
    movel(appro(l_pHere,trAppro),tAR70,mSlow)
    movej(jApera_Trigger_pos,tAR70,mSlow)
    movej(jHome,tAR70,mSlow)
    waitEndMove()
  elseIf _bEndFoV==true and l_pHere.trsf.z>-130
    restartMove()
    movej(jApera_Trigger_pos,tAR70,mSlow)
    movej(jHome,tAR70,mSlow)
    waitEndMove()
  endIf
  //
  //Robot outside of StartFoV and EndFoV
  //    Check if rejecting part. move to safe point then to home___CTC 12/11
  if _bStartFoV==false and _bEndFoV==false and bRejecting==true
    movej(appro(pReject_Station[1],{0,0,0,0,0,0}),tAR70,mSlow)
    waitEndMove()
    bRejecting=false
    movej(jHome,tAR70,mSlow)
    //call init()
  else
    // comment out CTC 12/11   //      Robot no moving once down on part... added MoveJHome to recover... CTC 8/8/24
    //    movej(jHome,tTool,mSlow)
    //    //
    //    wait(bRobotAtHome==true)
    //    call init()
  endIf
  call init()
  //Wait to Start or End Cycle Signal
  wait(bStart==true or bEndCycleRobot==true)

  if bEndCycleRobot
    call moveToHome()
  endIf
end]]></Code>
  </Program>

===== FILE: init.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="init" access="private" >
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_nScanHB" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nScanT0" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  This program performs all the initializations.
  //
  //  Main program sequence :
  //  - Initialization of cell Inputs/Outputs
  //  - Initialization of variables (num, bool, string, etc.)
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : June 2024 A. Cisneros (Stäubli)
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------

  //--------------------
  //  APERA init
  //--------------------
  //  tkBeltScan may own the single unlocked APERA socket right now: init() is
  //  reached from moveToHome() (End Cycle) and from clickAbort() - and in the
  //  abort case only AFTER clickAbort has already run its recovery motion, so
  //  by here anything could have happened. clearBuffer() on a socket a scan is mid-read on destroys the
  //  reply it is waiting for, so it must not run until the scan acknowledges.
  //
  //  Guarded by bScanForParts==true so this is a no-op at startup, when
  //  tkBeltScan does not exist yet and could never acknowledge - an unguarded
  //  wait here would hang the application on boot.
  //
  //  Watchdog on SILENCE (nScanHeartbeat), not on total time, and on expiry we
  //  SKIP the clearBuffer rather than doing it anyway: a wedged scan is
  //  probably stopped inside apera_send, and clearing the buffer under it is
  //  precisely the corruption this block exists to avoid. Stale bytes in the
  //  socket are recoverable; a garbage trajectory replayed into movej() is not.
  if bScanForParts==true
    bScanCancel=true
    l_nScanT0=clock()
    l_nScanHB=nScanHeartbeat
    while bScanDone==false and (clock()-l_nScanT0)<nScanTimeout_s
      if nScanHeartbeat!=l_nScanHB
        l_nScanHB=nScanHeartbeat
        l_nScanT0=clock()
      endIf
      delay(0.05)
    endWhile
    //  Give the hand-out back HERE, not with the other flags ~100 statements
    //  further down. Between this point and there sit tGripperEMH("OFF")
    //  (which itself delays) and ten userPageSet calls; leaving bScanForParts
    //  standing across all of that lets tkBeltScan re-enter its scan branch
    //  and issue fresh socket round-trips AFTER the clearBuffer below has
    //  already run.
    bScanForParts=false
    bScanCancel=false
  endIf
  //  Same conjunction every other site uses: bScanDone alone can read "free"
  //  during a claim that is still committing (tkCheckZone writes bScanDone
  //  false then bScanForParts true, and tkBeltScan's idle branch may set
  //  bScanDone true in between).
  //  Note the wait above is guarded on bScanForParts==true ONLY, deliberately:
  //  waiting on bScanDone at boot would hang the application, because
  //  tkBeltScan does not exist yet and nothing could acknowledge. The cost is
  //  that if bScanDone were ever persisted false from SP2, the first init()
  //  after a load skips the clearBuffer and logs once - then tkBeltScan's idle
  //  branch sets it true on its first pass and everything is normal again.
  if bScanDone==true and bScanForParts==false
    clearBuffer(apera:sAperaConn)
  else
    bOUTPUTS["Output3"]=false
    logMsg("RAIV/T1: init - APERA socket not confirmed free, NOT cleared (clearing under a live read would corrupt it)",1)
  endIf
  //--------------------
  //  INIT CELL OUTPUTS
  //--------------------
  //Robot Tool
  call tGripperEMH("OFF")
  if bDryCycle==false
    dDOValve1Enable=true
  else
    dDOValve1Enable=false
  endIf
  //
  //-----------------
  //  INIT I/O VALVES
  //-----------------
  dioSet(dDOAirStream,0)
  //
  //-----------------
  //  INIT VARIABLES
  //-----------------
  // Boolean variables
  bExit=false
  bDebug=true
  bRun=false
  bEndCycleRobot=false
  //bInZone=true
  bDangerRobMoved=false
  bIntervention=false
  bIntervMode=false
  bESKillInit=false
  bDryCycle=false
  bStart=false
  bAbort=false
  bPause=false
  bWarmUp=false
  bStow=false

  bOUTPUTS["Output1"]=false
  bOUTPUTS["Output2"]=false
  bOUTPUTS["Output3"]=false
  bOUTPUTS["Output4"]=false
  bOUTPUTS["Output5"]=false
  bOUTPUTS["Output6"]=false
  bOUTPUTS["Output7"]=false
  bOUTPUTS["Output8"]=false
  //
  // String variables
  sRobotStatus=""
  //+++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++set pipeline with PLC output CTC 6/24+++++ Moved to tkIOMonitor CTC 12/23
  //  if dioGet(dDI14in_tieplate)==1
  //    sPipeline="14in_tieplate"
  //  elseIf dioGet(dDI18in_tieplate)==1
  //    sPipeline="18in_tieplate"
  //  else
  //    sPipeline="Pick_IdsBelt"
  //    sTiePlateUser="Pandral"
  //  endIf
  //++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++CTC 6/24
  sFounds=""
  _sCycleTime["LastSec"]=""
  _sCycleTime["LastPPM"]=""
  _sCycleTime["AverageSec"]=""
  _sCycleTime["AveragePPM"]=""
  _sCycleTime["BestSec"]=""
  _sCycleTime["BestPPM"]=""
  _sCycleTime["WorseSec"]=""
  _sCycleTime["WorsePPM"]=""
  //
  //Numerical variables
  _nTimer=0
  for _nCycleTimeIndex=0 to size(_nCycleTime)-1
    _nCycleTime[_nCycleTimeIndex]=0
  endFor
  _nCycleTimeIndex=0
  //
  //--- Early belt advance: clear all latch/datum state ----------------------
  //  An abort between the gripper closing and the part lifting would otherwise
  //  leave bBeltLiftArmed standing, and a later unrelated move could satisfy
  //  the height test and start the conveyor. Everything except bEarlyBeltKick
  //  (the operator's enable, which is meant to persist) is cleared here.
  //  IMPORTANT, and the reason tkCheckZone's claim also tests bAbort: this
  //  clearing happens too LATE to cover the abort case on its own, because
  //  clickAbort runs stopMove/resetMotion and movej(jHome) - a lift far beyond
  //  76.2 mm - and only calls init() afterwards. The bAbort term in
  //  tkCheckZone is what actually prevents the kick firing on that recovery
  //  move; this is the belt-and-braces cleanup after it.
  bLastPart=false
  bBeltLiftArmed=false
  bEarlyKickFired=false
  bFeederStarved=false
  nBeltPickZ_mm=0
  nTCP_beltLift_mm=0
  nBeltArmClk=0
  nPickLiftSeq=0
  //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  //
  //--- Parallel belt scan: socket-ownership handshake -----------------------
  //  bScanDone is NOT assigned here, on purpose. It is the "APERA socket is
  //  free" acknowledgement, and its current value is already the truth: true
  //  if tkBeltScan acknowledged, or if no scan was ever running (the .dtx
  //  default is true, which is what makes the very first capture take the fast
  //  path); false if the scan is wedged. Forcing it true here would tell every
  //  later caller the socket is free at exactly the moment the handshake at the
  //  top of this program had just proved it is not.
  //  tkBeltScan's idle branch republishes it, so a stale false always heals.
  //
  //  nCapAtPlace / nCapDeferred are deliberately NOT reset either: init() runs
  //  from moveToHome() at end of cycle, which is exactly when you want to read
  //  them. They are plain globals, so they zero on reload.
  //
  //  With bEarlyBeltKick=false nothing ever sets bScanForParts, so all of this
  //  is inert and the cell reads exactly as it does today.
  bScanForParts=false
  bScanCancel=false
  //--- SENSOR-ONLY BELT, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) --------------------------
  //  bPickInFlight holds the conveyor off while APERA's planned grip point
  //  must not move. tkCycle sets and clears it, but tkCycle can be KILLED
  //  mid-pick - clickAbort() does exactly that - and a flag left true would
  //  keep the belt stopped for ever with no indication of why. init() runs on
  //  every start, every End Cycle and every Abort, so this is the one place
  //  that can guarantee it.
  //  nNoPickOnBeam is the belt master's nudge trigger; a count carried across
  //  a restart would nudge the belt on the first broken beam after a start.
  bPickInFlight=false
  nNoPickOnBeam=0
  bScanFoundParts=false
  bTrajTriggered=false
  //  v2.2.0: the Options-page buttons for these earlier experiments were
  //  removed, so force them OFF here - nothing on the pendant can show or
  //  change them any more.
  bCaptureOnFovClear=false
  bStallSample=false
  bEarlyBeltKick=false
  bTrajBeltOk=false
  nTrajBeltStarts=-1
  //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  //
  //-----------------
  //  INIT BUTTONS
  //-----------------
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnStart","style.backgroundColor","#B6B6B6")
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnStart","disabled",false)
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnPause","style.backgroundColor","#B6B6B6")
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnPause","disabled",false)
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnEndCycle","style.backgroundColor","#B6B6B6")
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnEndCycle","disabled",false)
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnAbort","style.backgroundColor","#B6B6B6")
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnAbort","disabled",false)
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnHoming","style.backgroundColor","#B6B6B6")
  userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnHoming","disabled",false)
  //
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: initRobot.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="initRobot" access="private" >
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  This program performs all the initializations for the robot.
  //
  //  Main program sequence :
  //  - Check if robot is in Auto/Remote Mode
  //  - Wait for MoveHold pressed
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : June 2024 A. Cisneros (Stäubli)
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Read working mode
  _nWorkingMode=workingMode(_nWorkingStatus)

  //
  // Enable arm power
  if (!isPowered() and _nWorkingMode==1) or (!isPowered() and _nWorkingMode==3)
    wait(isPowered())
    // Set to Move (programmed movement)
    _nWorkingMode=workingMode(_nWorkingStatus)
    if _nWorkingStatus!=0
      //
      while _nWorkingStatus!=0
        _nWorkingMode=workingMode(_nWorkingStatus)
        //
      endWhile
    endIf
  elseIf !isPowered() and _nWorkingMode==4
    wait((dioGet(dDIPowerEnable)==1) or bExit==true)
  endIf
  //

  // 
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: killAllTask.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="killAllTask" access="private" >
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  Kills all the tasks : "BeltScan", "checkZone", "HMI", "IOMonitor", "cycle", "RobotAtHome", "checkES",
  //
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : June 2024 A. Cisneros (Stäubli)
  //  Modified : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - kill "BeltScan" as well, and make
  //             the conveyor provably dead before power is disabled.
  //             Sep 2026: ONE task commands bOUTPUTS["Output3"] - tkBeltSensor.
  //             The belt-off write below is kept and is still correct: it runs
  //             AFTER that task has been killed, so there is nothing left to
  //             re-assert the output, and leaving a conveyor energised through
  //             a kill-all is not a thing to be clever about. The note below is
  //             the pre-2026 description and is kept for the history:
  //             THREE tasks command bOUTPUTS["Output3"]: tkCycle, the new
  //             tkBeltScan, and tkCheckZone (the early-kick lift watcher).
  //             All three are now killed BEFORE the belt is commanded off, so
  //             none of them can re-command it into the teardown - killing
  //             BeltScan or checkZone mid-flight leaves them no chance to run
  //             their own belt-off.
  //             Then the belt is dropped and IOMonitor, still alive, is given
  //             a pass to push that to the hardware; and after IOMonitor is
  //             killed the outputs are driven directly with dioSet, because
  //             from that point nothing copies bOUTPUTS to the terminals any
  //             more and a bOUTPUTS write alone would be a no-op.
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------

  //  Every belt commander first - BeltScan, checkZone and cycle - so that
  //  none of them can re-command the conveyor after we drop it below.
  if taskStatus("BeltScan")!=-1
    taskKill("BeltScan")
    wait(taskStatus("BeltScan")==-1)
  endIf
  if taskStatus("checkZone")!=-1
    taskKill("checkZone")
    wait(taskStatus("checkZone")==-1)
  endIf
  if taskStatus("cycle")!=-1
    taskKill("cycle")
    wait(taskStatus("cycle")==-1)
  endIf
  //  Safe to force here, and only here: BeltScan is confirmed dead
  //  (taskStatus==-1 above), so nothing can be inside apera_send. Anywhere
  //  else, forcing these instead of waiting for the acknowledgement is the
  //  arm-crash bug.
  bScanForParts=false
  bScanCancel=false
  bScanDone=true
  //  Command the conveyor off and give IOMonitor - still alive - a pass to
  //  push it to the hardware.
  bOUTPUTS["Output3"]=false
  delay(0.2)
  if taskStatus("HMI")!=-1
    taskKill("HMI")
    wait(taskStatus("HMI")==-1)
  endIf
  if taskStatus("IOMonitor")!=-1
    taskKill("IOMonitor")
    wait(taskStatus("IOMonitor")==-1)
  endIf
  if taskStatus("RobotAtHome")!=-1
    taskKill("RobotAtHome")
    wait(taskStatus("RobotAtHome")==-1)
  endIf
  //  BeltSensor is a pure observer with no belt authority (see its header),
  //  so it is safe to kill here with the other watchers rather than up with
  //  the belt commanders. Added Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) with the end-of-belt
  //  laser sensor - this routine is currently unreachable because bExit is
  //  never set true, but leaving one task out of it is exactly the sort of
  //  omission that surfaces the day bExit gets wired up.
  if taskStatus("BeltSensor")!=-1
    taskKill("BeltSensor")
    wait(taskStatus("BeltSensor")==-1)
  endIf
  if taskStatus("checkES")!=-1
    taskKill("checkES")
    wait(taskStatus("checkES")==-1)
  endIf
  //  IOMonitor is gone, so nothing copies bOUTPUTS to the terminals any more -
  //  a bOUTPUTS write here would be a no-op. Drive both conveyor outputs
  //  directly so the belt is off even if IOMonitor never got a pass above (it
  //  may already have been dead when this program was entered).
  //  [0] is belt power, [1] is the signal to the plant PLC. Both must drop.
  dioSet(dDOConveyorON[0],0)
  dioSet(dDOConveyorON[1],0)
  disablePower()
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: monitoring.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="monitoring" access="private" >
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //Description of program
  //  This program starts the application.
  //  It performs the following actions :
  //  - call init function
  //  - start parallel tasks
  //  - start main loop :
  //    - start secondary loop :
  //        - run cycle task
  //        - check intervention request
  //    - reset robot
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : June 2024 A. Cisneros (Stäubli)
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------

  //---------------------
  //  CALL INIT FUNCTION
  //---------------------
  call init()
  //-----------------------
  //  START PARALLEL TASKS
  //-----------------------
  // task : check security zone
  taskCreate "checkZone",35,tkCheckZone()
  wait(taskStatus("checkZone")==1)

  // task : HMI
  taskCreate "HMI",100,tkHMI()
  wait(taskStatus("HMI")==1)

  // task : IO Monitor
  taskCreate "IOMonitor",100,tkIOMonitor()
  wait(taskStatus("IOMonitor")==1)

  // task : RobotAtHome
  taskCreate "RobotAtHome",10,tkRobotAtHome()
  wait(taskStatus("RobotAtHome")==1)

  // task : BeltScan - RETIRED Sep 2026. It no longer scans and no longer
  //  touches the belt; it only holds the APERA socket handshake in its "free"
  //  state for init(), AperaOwnSocket() and killAllTask(), which are still
  //  written against that protocol. See its header before deleting it.
  //  The taskStatus==-1 guard is belt-and-braces only. Being honest about it:
  //  this taskCreate sits BEFORE the do..until main loop, so it runs once per
  //  monitoring() invocation and the loop never re-reaches it - and the four
  //  sibling taskCreates above are unguarded anyway, so a re-entry without
  //  killAllTask() would already have failed before reaching this line. The
  //  guard is harmless and cheap; it is not fixing a known path.
  if taskStatus("BeltScan")==-1
    taskCreate "BeltScan",35,tkBeltScan()
    wait(taskStatus("BeltScan")==1)
  endIf
  //--- BELT SENSOR AND BELT MASTER, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) -----------
  //  ***  THIS TASK NOW OWNS THE CONVEYOR.  ***  It is no longer an
  //  observer: it is the only code in the application that may command
  //  bOUTPUTS["Output3"] on, and it makes that decision every
  //  nBeltSensorPoll_s from the debounced beam state it computes in the
  //  same pass. If it is not running, the belt never starts - which is the
  //  fail-safe direction, and WaitBeltParts reports it as a dead heartbeat
  //  rather than waiting silently.
  //
  //  PRIORITY LEFT AT 10, deliberately and with a caveat. The task ran at
  //  10 with a 10 ms poll through the 11 Sep runs and its readings agreed
  //  with the confirm detect 26 times out of 26 with zero chatter, so the
  //  scheduling is demonstrably adequate for a 10 ms decision - that is
  //  evidence, not an argument about what the number means. It is NOT
  //  verified that 10 is the best choice now that the task commands an
  //  output rather than watching one. If the belt ever feels slow to stop
  //  after a plate reaches the beam, this number is the first thing to
  //  look at, and nBeltSensorPoll_s is the second.
  //
  //  Guarded the same way as BeltScan above so a re-entry into
  //  monitoring() cannot create a second copy - two copies would give both
  //  bBeltPartAtEnd and the belt output two writers, which is the exact
  //  thing this whole change exists to prevent.
  if taskStatus("BeltSensor")==-1
    taskCreate "BeltSensor",10,tkBeltSensor()
    wait(taskStatus("BeltSensor")==1)
  endIf
  //---------------------
  //  CALL INIT ROBOT
  //---------------------
  call initRobot()

  //------------------
  //  START MAIN LOOP
  //------------------
  do
    //Add conditions for ES and monitoring intervention
    do
      //---------------
      // RUN CYCLE TASK
      //---------------
      //if(taskStatus("cycle")==-1 and bStart==true and nCycleRemain>0)
      if (taskStatus("cycle")==-1 and bStart==true and sPipeline!="")
        //nCycleRemain=nCycle
        taskCreate "cycle",100,tkCycle()
        wait(taskStatus("cycle")==1)
      endIf
      //---------------
      // ABORT TASK
      //---------------
      if (taskStatus("cycle")==1 and bAbort==true)
        taskKill("cycle")
        wait(taskStatus("cycle")==-1)
        taskCreate "abort",100,clickAbort()
        wait(taskStatus("abort")==-1)
      endIf
      //---------------------------
      // CHECK INTERVENTION REQUEST
      //---------------------------
      //    if bIntervention==true
      //      // manage intervention
      //      call intervention()
      //    endIf
      //    delay(0)
    until (bDangerRobMoved==true or bEndCycleRobot==true or bAbort==true or bExit==true or bESKillInit==true)
  until (bExit)
  call killAllTask()
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: tGripperEMH.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="tGripperEMH" access="private" >
    <Parameters xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Param/1" >
      <Parameter name="x_sAction" type="string" use="value" xsi:type="element" dimensions="1" />
    </Parameters>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  Pulses the Schunk EMH084 electromagnet ON or OFF.
  //
  //  ***  THE TWO DWELLS ARE NOW SEPARATE  ***  (Sep 2026, Cowork/Claude)
  //  This used to serve both pulses from ONE number, nEMH_gripperDelay, so
  //  the grip and the release were forced to take the same time. There is no
  //  physical reason for that: making a magnet against a steel tie plate and
  //  breaking it are different events, and the release also has the plate's
  //  residual magnetism working against it. They are now:
  //      nEMH_onDelay   - the GRIP pulse
  //      nEMH_offDelay  - the RELEASE pulse
  //  Both ship at 0.5, which is exactly what nEMH_gripperDelay held, so this
  //  change is behaviour-neutral until the numbers are actually swept.
  //
  //  nEMH_gripperDelay is deliberately still declared in the .dtx and is now
  //  UNREFERENCED BY ANY CODE. It is kept as the record of what the single
  //  combined value was, so a sweep can always be put back. Changing it now
  //  does nothing - change nEMH_onDelay / nEMH_offDelay instead.
  //
  //  WHY THESE ARE THE SAFE NUMBERS TO TUNE. Together with nGripSettle in
  //  tkCycle they are ~1.35 s of a 4.66 s cycle - 29% - and none of the three
  //  is motion. Shortening them changes no path, no speed and no
  //  acceleration, so unlike a descriptor change they cannot throw a plate.
  //  What they CAN do is release too early or grip too weakly, so they are
  //  gated on a bench measurement, not on arithmetic:
  //    * step nEMH_onDelay  down 0.5 / 0.4 / 0.3 / 0.2 and pick 10 plates at
  //      each - watch for a plate that does not come, or comes and slips;
  //    * step nEMH_offDelay down the same way - watch for a plate that stays
  //      stuck to the magnet, or drops late.
  //  To confirm a step actually landed, read SegMagnetOn and SegMagnetOff in
  //  the SP2 DATA EDITOR (_sCycleTime keys) or in the CSV log, line F and line
  //  E respectively. They are NOT on the Cycle Timing user page - that page has
  //  room for six segment rows and there are now fourteen. Adding the rows is a
  //  layout change on a page that cannot be tested from here, so it was left
  //  alone deliberately.
  //  Be clear-eyed about what these two numbers are worth: each is bounded
  //  inside its own pulse, so SegMagnetOn will read ~nEMH_onDelay and
  //  SegMagnetOff ~nEMH_offDelay. They CONFIRM a sweep took effect; they do not
  //  diagnose anything. The number that carries information is SegDescend,
  //  which holds the head start the early dioSet buys during the descent.
  //
  //  ***  _nGripSyncClk  ***
  //  The waitEndMove() below is a synchronisation point that already existed,
  //  and it is where the queued motion actually finishes. Stamping the clock
  //  immediately after it lets the callers split their own timing into
  //  "waiting for the arm" and "waiting for the coil" WITHOUT adding a single
  //  new waitEndMove anywhere - which matters, because adding one would break
  //  a blend and change the motion. Part_Accept/Part_Reject read it to
  //  separate SegDescend from SegMagnetOff; tkCycle's gripper split uses the
  //  same three-stamp trick on the ON side, but from its own clock() readings -
  //  tkCycle does not read _nGripSyncClk. Written at exactly one place (below),
  //  read at exactly two (Part_Accept, Part_Reject), zeroed in
  //  ResetCycleStats. Every one of those is in the tkCycle task, and grep of
  //  the whole project confirms tGripperEMH has no other caller, so there is no
  //  cross-task race on it.
  //  It is a plain timestamp, written on every call including the dry-cycle
  //  path, and nothing behavioural reads it.
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : original (CTC)
  //  Sep 2026 : nEMH_gripperDelay split into nEMH_onDelay / nEMH_offDelay;
  //             _nGripSyncClk stamp added. No motion, speed or IO order
  //             changed - the dioSet sequence is byte-for-byte the original.
  //  Oct 2026 : AR70 (Magswitch) branch added so 1RT53 drives the AR70 the same
  //             way 2RT57 does: sGripper=="AR70" -> open(tAR70) on ON,
  //             close(tAR70) on OFF. Valve time comes from the tool data
  //             (ioOpen/ioClose = 0.25 s), NOT from nEMH_onDelay/offDelay.
  //             sGripper="EMH" runs the original Schunk dioSet pulses unchanged.
  //             waitEndMove() and the _nGripSyncClk stamp are shared by both.
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  waitEndMove()
  _nGripSyncClk=clock()
  if bDryCycle==false
    if sGripper=="AR70"
      switch x_sAction
        case "ON"
          open(tAR70)
        break
        case "OFF"
          close(tAR70)
        break
      endSwitch
    else
      //popUpMsg("Magnet Enabled",1)
      switch x_sAction
        case "ON"
          dioSet(dDOemh_gripperON,1)
          delay(nEMH_onDelay)
          dioSet(dDOemh_gripperON,0)
        break
        case "OFF"
          dioSet(dDOemh_gripperOFF,1)
          delay(nEMH_offDelay)
          dioSet(dDOemh_gripperOFF,0)
        break
      endSwitch
    endIf
  else
    //popUpMsg("Magnet Disabled",1)
  endIf
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

        dDOValve1Enable=true

===== FILE: tkBeltScan.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="tkBeltScan" access="private" >
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  RETIRED, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude). This task no longer scans, no longer
  //  touches the conveyor and no longer makes an APERA call. All it does is
  //  keep the socket-ownership handshake in its "free" state so that the code
  //  which still speaks that protocol keeps working.
  //
  //  ***  WHY IT IS STILL HERE AND NOT DELETED  ***
  //  Three other programs are written against this handshake:
  //      init.pgx          waits for bScanDone before it will clearBuffer
  //      AperaOwnSocket    returns "socket free" only on
  //                        bScanDone==true and bScanForParts==false
  //      killAllTask       forces the trio to the free state
  //  Deleting the task would leave those globals with no writer at all. They
  //  are ordinary publics, so they would hold whatever value the .dtx last
  //  saved - and bScanDone persisted false would make init() log "socket not
  //  confirmed free" and AperaOwnSocket refuse for ever, on a socket that is
  //  genuinely free. That is a permanent cell stall caused by deleting dead
  //  code, which is a bad way to find out.
  //  So the task stays, holds the contract, and does nothing else. Removing
  //  it properly means removing the handshake from those three programs
  //  first, and that is a separate change with its own testing.
  //
  //  ***  WHAT IT USED TO DO, AND WHY IT STOPPED  ***
  //  It ran the conveyor and polled the camera until plates appeared, then
  //  stopped the belt - the camera decided when the belt stopped. It was
  //  handed the belt by tkCheckZone's early kick, which fired on "the vision
  //  count says this is the last part".
  //  Both halves of that are gone. The conveyor is now started and stopped by
  //  the laser beam at the pick end of the belt and by nothing else; see the
  //  single-writer rule in tkBeltSensor.pgx. The early trajectory trigger
  //  this task also carried is gone with it: the earliest valid capture is
  //  now "the belt has stopped on a physical detection", which tkCycle waits
  //  for directly through WaitBeltParts.
  //
  //  ***  IF YOU ARE LOOKING FOR THE OLD CODE  ***
  //  It is in the previous application, binpick_TiePlates, which is still on
  //  the controller. Do not paste it back in here piecemeal - it contains
  //  seven belt writes and the whole point of this change is that there is
  //  exactly one.
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude)
  //  Sep 2026 : retired to a handshake keeper - sensor-only belt control.
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  while true
    //  Liveness heartbeat. AperaOwnSocket resets its watchdog whenever this
    //  moves, so it must advance on EVERY pass.
    //  The saturation reset is not cosmetic: VAL3 num is floating point, so
    //  past 2^24 the increment becomes a no-op (n+1==n) and the counter STOPS
    //  rather than wrapping. Every caller would then see permanent "silence".
    //  Only inequality between successive reads is ever tested, so resetting
    //  to 0 is safe.
    if nScanHeartbeat>1000000
      nScanHeartbeat=0
    else
      nScanHeartbeat=nScanHeartbeat+1
    endIf
    //  Nothing hands this task out any more, so bScanForParts should never be
    //  true. If something sets it - a leftover value saved into the .dtx, or
    //  a future edit - give it straight back rather than leaving the cell
    //  waiting for a scan that will never run.
    if bScanForParts==true
      bScanForParts=false
      logMsg("RAIV/T1: tkBeltScan is retired but was handed a scan request - returning it. Something still sets bScanForParts.",2)
    endIf
    if bScanCancel==true
      bScanCancel=false
    endIf
    //  THE CONTRACT: idle means the socket is free, so say so every pass.
    //  This is what stops "bScanForParts==false with bScanDone==false" from
    //  being an absorbing state.
    if bScanDone==false
      bScanDone=true
    endIf
    //  Diagnostic only - nothing reads it to make a decision.
    bScanFoundParts=false
    delay(0.1)
  endWhile
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: tkBeltSensor.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="tkBeltSensor" access="private" >
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_bBeltWas" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bDwelling" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bFirstEdge" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bGate" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bJogPending" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bLast" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bNudging" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bRaw" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bStarveLogged" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bWant" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nFlips" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nHoldClk" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nNoPickAtNudge" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nNudgeT0" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nNudged" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nRunClk" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nStable" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nStopClk" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_pHere" type="pointRx" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  END-OF-BELT LASER SENSOR  +  SOLE OWNER OF THE CONVEYOR.
  //
  //  Two jobs, deliberately in ONE task and ONE loop:
  //    1. debounce the through-beam laser across the pick end of the belt
  //       and publish the state, the edge counts and the timings;
  //    2. decide, from that state alone, whether the conveyor runs.
  //
  //  ***  THE RULE, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude), requested by Coty  ***
  //      BEAM CLEAR  -> the conveyor RUNS.
  //      BEAM BROKEN -> the conveyor STOPS.
  //  Nothing else decides. Not "this looked like the last part", not "APERA
  //  found no trajectory", not a plate count. Those inferences are what the
  //  11 Sep data showed to be unreliable: the pick pipeline reported a plate
  //  that was not on the belt (the one held on the magnet) on 180 of 180
  //  frames, so every belt decision derived from the count inherited that
  //  error. A photo-eye cannot make that mistake.
  //
  //  ***  WHY THIS TASK AND NOT A NEW ONE  ***
  //  The beam state and the belt command are now computed in the SAME pass of
  //  the SAME loop, so the command can never be made from a stale copy of the
  //  sensor. A separate controller task reading bBeltPartAtEnd would be one
  //  poll behind at best and arbitrarily behind if it were descheduled - on a
  //  conveyor that is a plate past the pick point.
  //
  //  ***  SINGLE-WRITER RULE - READ THIS BEFORE ADDING ANY BELT CODE  ***
  //  This task is the ONLY code in the application that may command the
  //  conveyor ON. Before this change bOUTPUTS["Output3"] was written from 28
  //  places across 7 programs and the belt's behaviour was the sum of all of
  //  them; that is why it was possible for End Cycle to leave the belt
  //  twitching and for a scan to switch the belt back on immediately after
  //  another branch had stopped it.
  //  Four programs may still write it FALSE, and only false, as fail-safe
  //  shutdown: init(), clickAbort(), killAllTask() and AperaOwnSocket()'s
  //  give-up path. An OFF write can never create motion, and this loop
  //  re-asserts the correct state within one poll anyway because it compares
  //  against the live global rather than its own idea of what it last wrote.
  //  If you are about to add a belt write somewhere else: don't. Publish a
  //  flag and let this task read it.
  //
  //  ***  WHAT GATES THE BELT BESIDES THE BEAM  ***
  //    bBeltMasterEnable  ships true. False parks the belt off - maintenance.
  //    bStart             the cell is running.
  //    bEndCycleRobot     End Cycle pressed -> belt off immediately.
  //    bAbort             Abort -> belt off immediately.
  //    bPickInFlight      tkCycle owns this flag. It is true from just before
  //                       the APPROACH until the RETREAT has been issued, i.e.
  //                       across the window in which APERA's planned grip
  //                       point must not move. The beam being clear during
  //                       that window means the scene's remaining plates are
  //                       upstream of the beam - exactly the plates the arm is
  //                       reaching for - so feeding then would slide the
  //                       target out from under a committed trajectory. That
  //                       is the Raiv-Lab over-torque failure, and it is the
  //                       one thing allowed to defer the rule above. It defers
  //                       it by at most one pick.
  //    bBeamBlockedAlarm  latched fault, see NUDGE below.
  //    nBeltClearHeight_mm  THE LIFT GATE. See below.
  //
  //  ***  THE LIFT GATE - THE BELT WAITS FOR HEIGHT, NOT FOR TIME  ***
  //  Requested by Coty, 22 Sep: the robot has to get the picked plate off the
  //  belt and clear of the next ones coming before the conveyor moves. The
  //  first version of this was a fixed dwell in seconds. It was replaced the
  //  same day, for a reason worth keeping written down:
  //
  //      TCP VELOCITY ON THIS CELL IS NOT A CONSTANT. It has already been
  //      changed during this project (mReach, mCycleVariable, the monitor
  //      speed selector), so any number of seconds that is correct today is
  //      silently wrong after the next speed change - and wrong in the unsafe
  //      direction, because a faster arm makes the dwell too long and a slower
  //      one makes it too short with no indication either way.
  //
  //  So the belt waits for the arm to have physically RISEN clear instead:
  //
  //      nTCP_beltLift_mm = TCP world z  -  the z this plate was gripped at
  //      belt may start when that reaches nBeltClearHeight_mm (ships 76.2 mm,
  //      i.e. 3 in exactly)
  //
  //  That is a measurement, not an assumption, and it tracks any speed change
  //  automatically.
  //
  //  ***  MEASURED FROM THE REAL GRIP HEIGHT  ***
  //  nBeltPickZ_mm is captured by tkCycle in the two statements after
  //  tGripperEMH("ON") returns - the true height THIS plate was gripped from.
  //  Do not switch this to a taught approach pose. On the Raiv-Lab cell that
  //  datum was both above the grip point AND rewritten per pick by the vision
  //  transform, which is what made the equivalent feature fire intermittently
  //  there.
  //
  //  ***  MILLIMETRES  ***
  //  l_pHere.trsf.z is mm, and nBeltClearHeight_mm is mm. Raiv-Lab shipped
  //  this once in metres (0.0762) and the threshold became 0.076 mm - i.e. no
  //  gate at all, satisfied on the first poll. If the belt starts the instant
  //  the gripper closes, that is the first thing to check.
  //
  //  ***  WHY bBeltLiftArmed IS IN THE TEST  ***
  //  Before the first pick of a run, nBeltPickZ_mm is whatever init() left (0),
  //  which is not a real belt height, so the subtraction is meaningless. The
  //  arm flag is set by tkCycle at every grip and cleared by init()/clickAbort,
  //  so "not armed" means "no plate has been gripped since the last reset" and
  //  the gate correctly stands down. After the first grip it stays set and the
  //  gate is governed purely by the live measurement, which is the intent.
  //  THIS TASK DOES NOT WRITE IT - tkCycle, init and clickAbort do, exactly as
  //  before. It is read here and nowhere else.
  //
  //  ***  WHAT IT REPLACES, AND WHAT IT DOES NOT  ***
  //  It supersedes bPickInFlight as the thing that actually governs the
  //  release. bPickInFlight drops when the RETREAT IS ISSUED, and movej only
  //  queues - so it said "released" while the arm was still at grip height.
  //  This says released when the arm has actually risen 3 in. bPickInFlight is
  //  kept because it covers the APPROACH as well, before any grip has happened.
  //
  //  ***  IF THE ARM NEVER RISES  ***
  //  The belt stays stopped. That is the correct fail-safe - an arm parked at
  //  grip height with a plate under it is not a cell that should be feeding -
  //  and it is visible as nBeltMasterState 7 with nBeltStartHolds not
  //  advancing. There is deliberately NO timeout that starts the belt anyway.
  //
  //  nBeltStartDelay_s SHIPS AT 0 and is now a secondary, inert escape hatch:
  //  a floor in seconds applied on top of the lift gate, for the case where the
  //  height proves necessary but not sufficient. Leave it at 0 unless you need
  //  it.
  //
  //  tkIOMonitor remains the actuator and keeps its own isPowered() and
  //  bAbort gate on the physical outputs, so this task requesting the belt is
  //  necessary but not sufficient for the conveyor to turn.
  //
  //  ***  THE CAPTURE HANDSHAKE  ***
  //  bBeltReadyForCapture goes true when, and only when:
  //      the beam is broken, the belt command is off, it has BEEN off for
  //      nBeltSettle_s, no nudge is running and no alarm is latched.
  //  tkCycle waits on that flag and then, separately, waits for the arm to be
  //  out of the conveyor camera's view (_bStartFoV==false) before capturing.
  //  The two conditions are kept separate on purpose: this task knows about
  //  the belt and nothing about where the arm is, and mixing them would put a
  //  robot-position dependency inside the belt controller.
  //
  //  ***  NUDGE, THEN ALARM - the deadlock this rule creates  ***
  //  Sensor-only control has exactly one deadlock: something breaks the beam
  //  that the pick pipeline cannot pick - a plate lying on its side, a plate
  //  half past the beam, a bolt, debris. Belt stopped, no trajectory, for
  //  ever, with no timeout anywhere because every timeout the cell used to
  //  have was attached to the count logic that has just been removed.
  //  So: tkCycle counts consecutive captures that found nothing pickable
  //  WHILE THE BEAM WAS BROKEN into nNoPickOnBeam. When that reaches
  //  nBeamNudgeAfter this task runs the belt for nBeamNudge_s to try to move
  //  the obstruction past the beam, up to nBeamNudgeMax times. If the count
  //  is still high after the last nudge, bBeamBlockedAlarm latches, the belt
  //  stops and stays stopped, and the cell needs an operator.
  //  The alarm clears when the gate drops (Stop, End Cycle or Abort), so the
  //  recovery is "clear the obstruction, then Start" with no reload.
  //
  //  ***  RUNAWAY CAP  ***
  //  "Beam clear -> run" has no natural end if the sensor freezes clear or the
  //  feeder is empty. nBeltRunMax_s (ships 120) bounds a single continuous run
  //  with no accepted break edge: bFeederStarved is raised at half that as an
  //  indication and the belt keeps feeding, which is what was asked for; at
  //  the full cap the belt stops and the alarm latches, because 120 s of
  //  feeding with nothing reaching the beam is a fault, not a slow batch (a
  //  normal advance measured 1-8 s on 11 Sep). Set nBeltRunMax_s to 0 to
  //  disable the cap entirely.
  //
  //  Wiring:  dDIBeltPartPresent -> J206_EtherCAT\Term_2_(D24403100)\%I7
  //  ***  CONFIRM THE ADDRESS on the SP2 I/O page before the first run.
  //
  //  ***  POLARITY  ***
  //  A through-beam sensor can be wired light-on or dark-on, NPN or PNP, so
  //  which electrical level means "plate present" is a property of the
  //  hardware. bBeltSensorInvert makes that a data-editor change:
  //    false -> input HIGH means plate present
  //    true  -> input LOW  means plate present
  //  Set it by watching nBeltSensorRaw (undebounced, uninverted) with the belt
  //  empty and then with a plate on the beam.
  //  ***  GETTING THIS BACKWARDS NOW MATTERS MORE THAN IT USED TO.  ***
  //  While this task was an observer, a wrong polarity produced wrong
  //  statistics. It now produces a conveyor that runs when it should stop.
  //  Check it, with the cell in manual, before the first automatic run.
  //
  //  ***  WHAT IT PUBLISHES  ***
  //    bBeltPartAtEnd   debounced logical beam state. Written LAST on every
  //                     edge, after the counters, so a reader that sees the
  //                     new state also sees consistent counters.
  //    bBeamClear       its complement, for the Options page lamp. DISPLAY
  //                     ONLY - do not read it in logic.
  //    bBeltRunning     what this task is commanding right now.
  //    bBeltReadyForCapture  see THE CAPTURE HANDSHAKE above.
  //    bBeamBlockedAlarm     latched fault, see NUDGE above.
  //    nBeltMasterState 0 gated (cell not running)   1 feeding
  //                     2 stopped, ready to capture  3 nudging
  //                     4 alarm                      5 holding for a pick
  //                     6 stopped, settling          7 start dwell
  //    nBeltStartHolds  how many times the dwell has delayed a belt start.
  //                     One per episode, not per poll. It should track the
  //                     pick count almost exactly - if it does not, the dwell
  //                     is not firing where you think it is.
  //    nBeltSensorEdges accepted beam-BREAK edges. See THE LEVEL TRAP.
  //    nBeltSensorClears accepted beam-CLEAR edges.
  //    nBeltAtEnd       bBeltPartAtEnd as 0/1, because VAL3's toString() takes
  //                     a num and passing it a bool is a COMPILE ERROR - that
  //                     has already cost this project two build failures - and
  //                     LogCycleCSV has to print it.
  //    nBeltSensorRaw   undebounced raw input as 0/1, for aiming.
  //    nBeltSensorChatter  see CHATTER below.
  //    nBeltDwell_s     how long the last plate sat on the beam. WALL CLOCK -
  //                     read the MEDIAN across a run, never the mean.
  //    nBeltRun_s       how long the belt RAN to bring this plate up, measured
  //                     from belt-on, not from the previous plate clearing.
  //    nBeltBatchNo     belt-on count since the last reload. Identity, not a
  //                     statistic - nothing resets it.
  //    nBeltNudges / nBeltRunawayStops / nBeltMasterStarts   fault and
  //                     activity counters for the log.
  //    nBeltSensorHeartbeat  liveness, saturation-reset at 1e6 because VAL3
  //                     num is floating point and stops counting cleanly at
  //                     2^24 rather than wrapping.
  //
  //  ***  THE LEVEL TRAP - why the edge count is still published  ***
  //  Anything the pick pipeline does not recognise - a plate lying wrong, a
  //  bolt, a bird - occludes the beam permanently, and a pure level test then
  //  reads "plate present" for ever. The belt control above IS a level test,
  //  which is why the NUDGE and ALARM exist: they are the bounded way out
  //  that a level test does not have on its own. The edge counters remain
  //  published because they are how the log distinguishes "stopped because a
  //  plate arrived" from "stopped because something is sitting on the beam".
  //
  //  ***  DEBOUNCE, COUNTED IN POLLS NOT SECONDS  ***
  //  The raw state must hold for nBeltSensorStable_n consecutive polls (ships
  //  2, i.e. ~20 ms at the default 10 ms poll) before the published state
  //  follows it. Counted in POLLS on purpose: VAL3 num is single precision, so
  //  once clock() reaches ~1e6 s - eleven days of controller uptime, which
  //  this cell will see - the spacing between representable values is ~0.06 s
  //  and a 0.02 s threshold in seconds is no longer a filter, it is a coin
  //  flip on quantisation. A poll counter has no such failure mode.
  //
  //  ***  CHATTER  ***
  //  If the raw state flips faster than the stability window it never settles,
  //  no edge is accepted, and the published state FREEZES. Frozen "clear" now
  //  runs the belt and frozen "broken" now stops it, so chatter is detected
  //  rather than assumed away: nBeltChatterFlips (ships 20) raw flips with no
  //  accepted edge increments nBeltSensorChatter and logs once per episode.
  //  If that counter moves, the sensor is dirty, misaimed or picking up belt
  //  vibration, and the belt is being commanded from a frozen reading. The
  //  runaway cap bounds the "frozen clear" case; the nudge and alarm bound
  //  the "frozen broken" case. Neither is a substitute for fixing the sensor.
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - phase 1, observation only.
  //  Sep 2026 : nBeltBatchNo added.
  //  Sep 2026 : THIS TASK BECAME THE SOLE OWNER OF THE CONVEYOR. The belt is
  //             now controlled by the beam alone; bLastPart, the early belt
  //             kick and the "no trajectory found" belt runs are gone from the
  //             application. See the BeltSensor README shipped with this
  //             application, and the single-writer rule above.
  //  Oct 2026 v2.1.0 (Cowork/Claude): JOG, DO NOT LATCH. A miss on a broken
  //             beam now jogs the belt nBeamNudge_s (1.0 s) after
  //             nBeamNudgeAfter (1) miss, every miss, with no limit while
  //             nBeamNudgeMax is 0. bBeamJogWarn + a level-2 message every
  //             nBeamNudgeWarn (5) jogs in a row. nBeamNudgeMax>0 restores
  //             the old "N jogs then alarm" behaviour.
  //  Oct 2026 v2.2.0 (Cowork/Claude): CAPTURE-READY RACE FIXED. l_nStopClk is
  //             stamped in the same pass that switches the belt off, so the
  //             nBeltSettle_s wait is always honoured. nBeltSettle_s now ships
  //             1.0 s: the belt is hydraulic and coasts a load-dependent
  //             distance. Set it to the measured fully-loaded stop time.
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //  Seed from the CURRENT input state rather than from false, so that a plate
  //  already sitting on the beam at start-up is not reported as a fresh break
  //  edge the first time round the loop.
  l_bRaw=(dioGet(dDIBeltPartPresent)==1)
  if bBeltSensorInvert==true
    l_bRaw=(l_bRaw==false)
  endIf
  l_bLast=l_bRaw
  bBeltPartAtEnd=l_bRaw
  bBeamClear=(l_bRaw==false)
  if l_bRaw==true
    nBeltAtEnd=1
  else
    nBeltAtEnd=0
  endIf
  l_nStable=0
  l_nFlips=0
  //  The first accepted edge has no meaningful "previous" to measure from, so
  //  its timings are published as 0 rather than as time-since-start-up.
  l_bFirstEdge=true
  l_bBeltWas=(bOUTPUTS["Output3"]==true)
  _nBeltBreakClk=clock()
  _nBeltRunClk=clock()
  //  Belt-master state. Seeded to "stopped just now" so the settle timer has
  //  to elapse before the first capture is allowed, exactly as it would after
  //  any ordinary stop.
  l_bDwelling=false
  l_bNudging=false
  l_bStarveLogged=false
  l_nNudged=0
  l_nNoPickAtNudge=0
  l_nNudgeT0=clock()
  l_nStopClk=clock()
  l_nRunClk=clock()
  l_nHoldClk=clock()
  nTCP_beltLift_mm=0
  bBeltRunning=(bOUTPUTS["Output3"]==true)
  bBeltReadyForCapture=false
  nBeltMasterState=0
  //
  while true
    if nBeltSensorHeartbeat>1000000
      nBeltSensorHeartbeat=0
    else
      nBeltSensorHeartbeat=nBeltSensorHeartbeat+1
    endIf
    //=====================================================================
    //  PART 1 - READ THE SENSOR
    //=====================================================================
    //--- RUNAWAY TIMER ARMING - Sep 2026 FIX (Cowork/Claude) ---------------
    //  ***  THIS IS THE LINE THAT KEPT THE CONVEYOR FROM EVER STARTING.  ***
    //  l_nRunClk was stamped once at task start and then only on a belt
    //  off->on edge, so while the belt was OFF the timer kept running. The
    //  task is created when the APPLICATION loads; the operator presses Start
    //  some minutes later. On the very first pass after Start the cap saw
    //  clock()-l_nRunClk >= nBeltRunMax_s - time spent sitting at the pendant,
    //  not time spent feeding - and latched bBeamBlockedAlarm BEFORE the belt
    //  had run for a single poll.
    //  It was self-reinforcing, which is why it looked like dead hardware:
    //  the alarm clears when the gate drops, but l_nRunClk only advanced on a
    //  belt off->on edge, and there could never be one, so the next Start
    //  re-latched it instantly.
    //  The cap is meant to bound ONE CONTINUOUS RUN. So the timer is reset on
    //  every pass in which the belt is not energised, and accumulates only
    //  while it is. Read from the LIVE output, so a fail-safe OFF written by
    //  init/clickAbort/killAllTask re-arms it too.
    if bOUTPUTS["Output3"]==false
      l_nRunClk=clock()
      l_bStarveLogged=false
    endIf
    //--- belt off -> on: this is where "how long the belt ran" starts -------
    if bOUTPUTS["Output3"]==true and l_bBeltWas==false
      _nBeltRunClk=clock()
      //  Redundant with the re-arm above (the previous pass had the belt off,
      //  so both were just reset) and kept deliberately: it is the statement
      //  that says "a continuous run starts HERE", and a future edit that
      //  moves the re-arm must not silently take that meaning with it.
      l_nRunClk=clock()
      l_bStarveLogged=false
      //  Saturating rather than wrapping is deliberate. VAL3 num is single
      //  precision, so integers are exact only to 2^24; past that +1 is a
      //  no-op and the counter STOPS. Saturation MERGES batches in the
      //  analysis, which is visible and harmless; a wrap would make batch 3
      //  appear twice in one log and silently scramble the grouping.
      if nBeltBatchNo<16000000
        nBeltBatchNo=nBeltBatchNo+1
      endIf
      if nBeltMasterStarts<16000000
        nBeltMasterStarts=nBeltMasterStarts+1
      endIf
    endIf
    //--- belt on -> off: stamp the settle anchor ---------------------------
    if bOUTPUTS["Output3"]==false and l_bBeltWas==true
      l_nStopClk=clock()
    endIf
    l_bBeltWas=(bOUTPUTS["Output3"]==true)
    //--- raw read, published before polarity is applied --------------------
    l_bRaw=(dioGet(dDIBeltPartPresent)==1)
    if l_bRaw==true
      nBeltSensorRaw=1
    else
      nBeltSensorRaw=0
    endIf
    //--- polarity ----------------------------------------------------------
    if bBeltSensorInvert==true
      l_bRaw=(l_bRaw==false)
    endIf
    //--- debounce, counted in consecutive stable polls ----------------------
    if l_bRaw!=l_bLast
      l_bLast=l_bRaw
      l_nStable=0
      //  Only a flip that happens while the state is UNSETTLED counts toward
      //  chatter. A clean break followed later by a clean clear is two
      //  accepted edges, not chatter, and each resets the counter below.
      l_nFlips=l_nFlips+1
      if l_nFlips>=nBeltChatterFlips
        nBeltSensorChatter=nBeltSensorChatter+1
        l_nFlips=0
        logMsg("RAIV/T1: belt sensor CHATTERING - published state is frozen and the CONVEYOR IS BEING COMMANDED FROM IT. Clean/re-aim the sensor now.",3)
      endIf
    else
      l_nStable=l_nStable+1
    endIf
    //--- accept the edge once it has been stable long enough ---------------
    if l_bRaw!=bBeltPartAtEnd and l_nStable>=nBeltSensorStable_n
      if l_bRaw==true
        //  BEAM BROKEN - a plate has arrived at the end of the belt.
        if l_bFirstEdge==true
          nBeltRun_s=0
        else
          nBeltRun_s=clock()-_nBeltRunClk
        endIf
        _nBeltBreakClk=clock()
        nBeltSensorEdges=nBeltSensorEdges+1
        nBeltAtEnd=1
        //  A plate reached the beam, so the feed is working: clear the
        //  starvation indication and re-arm the runaway cap.
        bFeederStarved=false
        l_bStarveLogged=false
        l_nRunClk=clock()
      else
        //  BEAM CLEAR - the plate has been picked (or pushed past).
        if l_bFirstEdge==true
          nBeltDwell_s=0
        else
          nBeltDwell_s=clock()-_nBeltBreakClk
        endIf
        nBeltSensorClears=nBeltSensorClears+1
        nBeltAtEnd=0
      endIf
      l_bFirstEdge=false
      //  A clean accepted edge is the evidence that the input is NOT
      //  chattering, so the flip budget resets here and nowhere else.
      l_nFlips=0
      //  Published LAST. See the note in the header.
      bBeltPartAtEnd=l_bRaw
      bBeamClear=(l_bRaw==false)
    endIf
    //=====================================================================
    //  PART 2 - COMMAND THE BELT
    //  Everything below reads the state just computed above, in the same
    //  pass. No other task may write bOUTPUTS["Output3"] true.
    //=====================================================================
    //--- HOW FAR HAS THE ARM RISEN SINCE IT GRIPPED?  ----------------------
    //  Published as well as used, so the existing CSV line D <liftMm> column
    //  comes back to life and the gate can be watched from the data editor.
    //  SOLE WRITER of nTCP_beltLift_mm - tkCycle's own zeroing of it was
    //  removed with this change so there is one owner.
    l_pHere=here(tAR70,world)
    nTCP_beltLift_mm=l_pHere.trsf.z-nBeltPickZ_mm
    l_bGate=(bBeltMasterEnable==true and bStart==true and bEndCycleRobot==false and bAbort==false)
    //--- SECONDARY TIME FLOOR - ARMING ------------------------------------
    //  nBeltStartDelay_s ships 0, so this is inert. It is kept as an escape
    //  hatch (see the header) and it follows the same discipline as every
    //  other timer in this task: RE-ARMED on every pass in which any reason to
    //  hold the belt is present, never stamped once. The 22 Sep runaway defect
    //  was a timer stamped once that then measured idle time as run time; it
    //  latched an alarm before the belt had ever run.
    //  The lift gate is in this list too, so the floor - if it is ever enabled
    //  - runs from the arm being clear, not from the beam edge.
    if l_bGate==false or bBeltPartAtEnd==true or bPickInFlight==true or bBeamBlockedAlarm==true or (bBeltLiftArmed==true and nTCP_beltLift_mm<nBeltClearHeight_mm)
      l_nHoldClk=clock()
      l_bDwelling=false
    endIf
    //  Nudge budget follows tkCycle's counter: the moment a pick succeeds
    //  tkCycle zeroes nNoPickOnBeam and the budget comes back. This task
    //  never writes that counter - one owner per flag.
    if nNoPickOnBeam==0
      l_nNudged=0
      l_nNoPickAtNudge=0
      bBeamJogWarn=false
    endIf
    l_bWant=false
    if l_bGate==false
      //  Cell not running. Belt off, nudge state discarded, and the alarm
      //  cleared so that "clear the obstruction, then Start" is the whole
      //  recovery - no reload, no data-editor visit.
      l_bNudging=false
      l_nNudged=0
      l_nNoPickAtNudge=0
      bBeamBlockedAlarm=false
      bBeamJogWarn=false
      bFeederStarved=false
      nBeltMasterState=0
    elseIf bBeamBlockedAlarm==true
      nBeltMasterState=4
    elseIf l_bNudging==true
      //  A nudge is in progress. It runs the belt against a broken beam on
      //  purpose, so it is time-bounded and cannot be extended by anything.
      if (clock()-l_nNudgeT0)<nBeamNudge_s and bPickInFlight==false
        l_bWant=true
        nBeltMasterState=3
      else
        l_bNudging=false
        nBeltMasterState=6
      endIf
    elseIf bBeltPartAtEnd==false
      //=================================================================
      //  BEAM CLEAR -> FEED.  This is the rule.
      //=================================================================
      if bPickInFlight==true
        //  Deferred by at most one pick - see bPickInFlight in the header.
        nBeltMasterState=5
      elseIf (bBeltLiftArmed==true and nTCP_beltLift_mm<nBeltClearHeight_mm) or (clock()-l_nHoldClk)<nBeltStartDelay_s
        //--- THE LIFT GATE, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude), requested by Coty -----
        //  Hold the belt until the arm has carried the picked plate
