        //  nBeltClearHeight_mm (76.2 mm = 3 in) above the height it gripped
        //  it from. Height, not seconds - TCP velocity on this cell has
        //  already changed once and will again. See THE LIFT GATE in the
        //  header, including the millimetres warning.
        //  The second term is the inert time floor (nBeltStartDelay_s ships 0).
        //  Counted once per episode rather than once per poll - at a 10 ms
        //  poll the per-poll count would be the hold length in centiseconds
        //  and would tell you nothing.
        nBeltMasterState=7
        if l_bDwelling==false
          l_bDwelling=true
          if nBeltStartHolds<16000000
            nBeltStartHolds=nBeltStartHolds+1
          endIf
        endIf
      else
        l_bWant=true
        nBeltMasterState=1
        //--- runaway cap ------------------------------------------------
        //  Bounds ONE continuous run with no accepted break edge.
        if nBeltRunMax_s>0
          if (clock()-l_nRunClk)>=(nBeltRunMax_s/2) and l_bStarveLogged==false
            l_bStarveLogged=true
            bFeederStarved=true
            logMsg("RAIV/T1: conveyor has run "+toString("",nBeltRunMax_s/2)+"s with nothing reaching the beam - feeder starved. Still feeding.",2)
          endIf
          if (clock()-l_nRunClk)>=nBeltRunMax_s
            l_bWant=false
            bBeamBlockedAlarm=true
            nBeltRunawayStops=nBeltRunawayStops+1
            nBeltMasterState=4
            logMsg("RAIV/T1: conveyor RUNAWAY STOP - ran "+toString("",nBeltRunMax_s)+"s with no plate at the beam. Check the feeder and the sensor, then press Start.",3)
          endIf
        endIf
      endIf
    else
      //=================================================================
      //  BEAM BROKEN -> STOP.  Also the rule.
      //=================================================================
      nBeltMasterState=6
      //  Start a nudge only if tkCycle has told us the thing on the beam is
      //  not pickable, and only between picks.
      //  nNoPickOnBeam>l_nNoPickAtNudge is what makes the nudges SEQUENTIAL
      //  rather than back-to-back: after a nudge, tkCycle must capture again
      //  and fail again before another nudge is allowed. Without it all
      //  nBeamNudgeMax nudges fire in consecutive polls, the belt runs for
      //  nBeamNudgeMax*nBeamNudge_s continuously against a broken beam, and
      //  the alarm latches without any of the nudges ever having been given a
      //  chance to work.
      if nNoPickOnBeam>=nBeamNudgeAfter and nNoPickOnBeam>l_nNoPickAtNudge and bPickInFlight==false
        if nBeamNudgeMax<=0 or l_nNudged<nBeamNudgeMax
          l_bNudging=true
          l_nNudged=l_nNudged+1
          l_nNoPickAtNudge=nNoPickOnBeam
          l_nNudgeT0=clock()
          nBeltNudges=nBeltNudges+1
          l_bWant=true
          nBeltMasterState=3
          logMsg("RAIV/T1: nothing pickable on a broken beam - jogging the belt "+toString("",nBeamNudge_s)+"s, jog "+toString("",l_nNudged)+" in a row",1)
          //  v2.1.0: warn (never stop) every nBeamNudgeWarn jogs in a row with
          //  no successful pick. Something unpickable is probably riding the belt.
          if nBeamNudgeWarn>0
            if (l_nNudged%nBeamNudgeWarn)==0
              bBeamJogWarn=true
              logMsg("RAIV/T1: belt jogged "+toString("",l_nNudged)+" times in a row with nothing pickable at the sensor - check the belt for a flipped plate or debris. Still jogging.",2)
            endIf
          endIf
        else
          //  Budget spent AND a further capture has failed since the last
          //  nudge, so the nudges demonstrably did not clear it.
          bBeamBlockedAlarm=true
          nBeltMasterState=4
          logMsg("RAIV/T1: BEAM BLOCKED - something on the beam cannot be picked and "+toString("",nBeamNudgeMax)+" nudges did not clear it. Belt stopped. Clear the belt, then press Start.",3)
        endIf
      endIf
    endIf
    //--- the one write -----------------------------------------------------
    //  Compared against the LIVE global, not against a local copy, so that a
    //  fail-safe OFF written by init/clickAbort/killAllTask/AperaOwnSocket is
    //  detected and the correct state re-asserted within one poll instead of
    //  this task believing it had already commanded it.
    if bOUTPUTS["Output3"]!=l_bWant
      //  v2.2.0: stamp the stop time IN THE PASS that commands the stop. It used
      //  to be stamped by the edge detector at the top of the NEXT pass, so the
      //  capture-ready test below ran once against the PREVIOUS stop time and
      //  raised bBeltReadyForCapture for one poll at the instant the belt was
      //  told to stop - a capture could fire on a still-moving belt.
      if l_bWant==false
        l_nStopClk=clock()
      endIf
      bOUTPUTS["Output3"]=l_bWant
    endIf
    bBeltRunning=l_bWant
    //--- the capture handshake ---------------------------------------------
    //  Deliberately NOT a function of where the arm is. tkCycle adds the
    //  _bStartFoV term itself; see THE CAPTURE HANDSHAKE in the header.
    //  v2.1.0: a miss that has not been jogged for yet blocks the capture,
    //  so tkCycle never photographs the same unpickable scene twice.
    l_bJogPending=(nNoPickOnBeam>=nBeamNudgeAfter and nNoPickOnBeam>l_nNoPickAtNudge)
    if l_bGate==true and bBeltPartAtEnd==true and l_bWant==false and l_bNudging==false and l_bJogPending==false and bBeamBlockedAlarm==false and (clock()-l_nStopClk)>=nBeltSettle_s
      bBeltReadyForCapture=true
      if nBeltMasterState==6
        nBeltMasterState=2
      endIf
    else
      bBeltReadyForCapture=false
    endIf
    delay(nBeltSensorPoll_s)
  endWhile
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: tkCheckZone.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="tkCheckZone" access="private" >
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_bArea1" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bArea2" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bAreaError" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //Initialize Areas
  call monitorArea:init()
  //
  //Create areas
  //  ***  THE BOUNDARY MOVED FROM +310 TO +550, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude)  ***
  //  The two cubes are not two halves of the camera's view - the original
  //  comments below say what they are, and they are right: cube 1 is "the
  //  gripper is IN the APERA view", cube 2 is "the gripper is PAST it". They
  //  meet at a single boundary, and everything that waits for a clear shot
  //  waits for _bStartFoV to go false at that boundary.
  //
  //  THE BOUNDARY WAS IN THE WRONG PLACE. Cube 1 ran x -525..+310, so
  //  _bStartFoV went false at x=+310. Measured at the cell, the gripper and
  //  the plate it is holding do not fully clear the camera until about
  //  x=+550. So for 240 mm of travel the flag said "clear" while the camera
  //  could still see the plate on the magnet - and every capture taken in
  //  that window images the held plate as if it were a plate on the belt.
  //
  //  The evidence, from the APERA recordings of run 20: at capture positions
  //  x=326..443 the pick pipeline reports a plate that has no valid pick
  //  point, on 103 of 105 frames, INCLUDING two frames (111, 137) where the
  //  belt was provably empty and the count still came back 1 rather than 0.
  //  Stereo disparity on frame 111 puts that object 0.39 m from the camera
  //  against 1.10 m for the belt - i.e. 0.71 m up in the air, on the magnet.
  //  That is the phantom that keeps nCountPlatesFound off 0, which stops
  //  bLastPart arming the early belt kick, which sends 34 of 63 belt
  //  advances through Branch B at 3.36 s each: +0.56 s on every pick.
  //
  //  nStartFoVX_mm SHIPS AT 1075 (-525 + 1075 = +550). This is a DELIBERATE
  //  behaviour change, requested with a measured number - it is not one of
  //  the inert toggles. Setting it back to 835 restores the old boundary
  //  exactly.
  //  IT IS READ ONCE, HERE, AT TASK START. Changing it from the data editor
  //  does not resize a cube that already exists - restart the application.
  //  If you change it and do not restart, Part_Accept's geometry self-check
  //  disagrees with the real flag and nFovGeomMismatch counts every poll, so
  //  the log tells you rather than letting the numbers quietly rot.
  //APERA IN Field of View
  call monitorArea:createCube(pStartCnvyrFoV,nStartFoVX_mm,835,1000,l_bAreaError)
  //APERA OUT Field of View
  //  Anchored at pEndCnvyrFoV, whose x moves 310 -> 550 with the boundary so
  //  the two zones still meet and do not overlap. Width follows (600 -> 360)
  //  so the far face stays where it was at +910. Nothing gates motion on
  //  _bEndFoV - it feeds nFovHitEnd and the bRejecting clear below - so this
  //  half is bookkeeping, but a zone that claims "past the camera" while the
  //  camera can still see you is exactly the lie being fixed here.
  call monitorArea:createCube(pEndCnvyrFoV,nEndFoVX_mm,800,1030,l_bAreaError)
  //
  //Check if there is an error to in the creating of the areas
  if l_bAreaError
    logMsg("Error Creating AREAS")
  endIf
  //
  while true
    //Check if robot is inside of APERA Field of View
    call monitorArea:checkCube(tAR70,1,l_bArea1,l_bAreaError)
    if l_bAreaError==true
      logMsg("Error Area 1")
    endIf
    if l_bArea1==true
      _bStartFoV=true
    else
      _bStartFoV=false
    endIf
    //
    //Check if robot is outside of APERA Field of View
    call monitorArea:checkCube(tAR70,2,l_bArea2,l_bAreaError)
    if l_bAreaError==true
      logMsg("Error Area 1")
    endIf
    if l_bArea2==true
      _bEndFoV=true
    else
      _bEndFoV=false
    endIf
    //
    //____________Turn off Rejecting bool CTC 12/13
    if _bEndFoV==true or _bStartFoV==true
      bRejecting=false
    endIf
    //
    //=====================================================================
    //  EARLY BELT ADVANCE - REMOVED, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude)
    //=====================================================================
    //  This task used to watch for the robot lifting what the vision count
    //  had called the LAST part and start the conveyor at that instant. The
    //  whole block is gone, and with it this task's only write to
    //  bOUTPUTS["Output3"] and its only claim on the APERA socket.
    //
    //  WHY, in one line: the count it depended on was wrong in a way that
    //  could not be tuned out. On 11 Sep the pick pipeline reported a mean of
    //  4.12 plates where 2.98 were pickable, and never once reported zero in
    //  180 frames - it was counting the plate held on the magnet as a plate on
    //  the belt. So "this is the last part" fired on 20 of 35 belt advances
    //  and missed the rest, and there was no threshold that fixed it.
    //  The conveyor is now started by the laser beam at the pick end of the
    //  belt and by nothing else. See tkBeltSensor.pgx.
    //
    //  WHAT IT COST TO REMOVE, honestly: on 11 Sep the kick was worth about
    //  2.6-2.9 s per belt advance, all of it from overlapping the belt run
    //  with the place-and-return move. That overlap is NOT lost - tkCycle
    //  releases bPickInFlight as soon as the retreat is issued, so the belt
    //  master starts feeding across the same window. What is lost is the
    //  ability to start the belt BEFORE the plate leaves the beam, which the
    //  beam cannot know about. Expect the advance itself to start roughly one
    //  beam-clear later than the kick managed.
    //
    //  The globals this block used - bBeltLiftArmed, bEarlyBeltKick,
    //  bEarlyKickFired, nBeltPickZ_mm, nBeltClearHeight_mm, nBeltArmClk,
    //  nBeltKickTimeout_s, nTCP_beltLift_mm, nPickLiftSeq - are LEFT IN THE
    //  .dtx and left bound to the Options page. Nothing writes or reads them
    //  any more. They are kept so that the historical CSV columns still parse
    //  and so that a reader of the Options page sees a switch that plainly
    //  does nothing rather than a missing global and a broken binding.
    //  bEarlyBeltKick now ships false and has no effect either way.
    //
    //  delay(0) added with the removal above. This loop has never had a delay,
    //  and that was measured as safe - but it was measured with the ~60-line
    //  kick block in it. Deleting that makes the loop spin faster and take a
    //  larger share of the scheduler, and the task it would take it from is
    //  tkBeltSensor, which now has to make a conveyor decision every 10 ms.
    //  delay(0) yields without adding wall time, so the _bStartFoV latency
    //  that Part_Accept's 20 ms poll depends on is unchanged.
    delay(0)
  endWhile
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: tkCycle.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8"?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2">
  <Program name="tkCycle">
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_InPickProcess" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bBeltReady" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bCycleDone" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bEnding" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bFovClear" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bKickPath" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bSocketFree" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bTrajectoryFound" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nAlarmClk" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nBrDet" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nBrSettle" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nBrT0" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nBrT1" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nBrT2" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nBrT3" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nClassesFound" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nElapsed" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nLPThr" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nLoopTopT0" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nMoveID" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nNOTrajectoryFound" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nPickPoseID" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nSegI" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nSegPF" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nSegT0" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nSegT1" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nSegT2" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nTries" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_pDropPos" type="pointRx" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_pHere" type="pointRx" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_pPose" type="pointRx" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_pPoseTransform" type="pointRx" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_sClassID" type="string" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_sPipeline" type="string" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_sPipelineGroup" type="string" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  Controls robot movements. Gives the cycle time.
  //  Steps:
  //  1) Tie Plate Selection from HMI, waits until Operator selects a pipeline
  //  2) First Cycle: 
  //     * Move robot outside the field of view for an unobstructed camera capture. 
  //     * Then Capture a image and wait for trajectory.
  //  3) In Loop: 
  //     * Check conveyor is OFF before Pick a part.
  //     * Tie Plate Selection.
  //     * Move robot outside the field of view for an unobstructed camera capture. 
  //     * Then Capture a image and wait for trajectory. If there isn't trajectory move away camera view and recapture a image.
  //  4) Execute Trajectory from APERA Auto Pilot:
  //     * IF conveyor is stop continue to drop part.
  //     * IF conveyor is running wait unitl stops and move away camera view and capture a image.
  //  5) Drop Part
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : June 2024 A. Cisneros (Stäubli)
  //  Modified : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - END CYCLE BELT FIX. The conveyor
  //             behaved differently on End Cycle depending on which detector
  //             branch the cycle happened to be in, because:
  //               * the "No Pickable Parts Found" branch had NO bEndCycleRobot
  //                 check at all and turned the belt on regardless;
  //               * moveToHome() ends with call init(), and init() clears
  //                 bEndCycleRobot (and bStart, and Output3) - so calling it
  //                 from inside the detector loop wiped the very flag the loop
  //                 was testing, mid-loop;
  //               * tkIOMonitor re-asserts bEndCycleRobot=true on every pass
  //                 for as long as the End Cycle input reads active, so the
  //                 flag could come back after init() cleared it.
  //             Whether the belt ran therefore depended on a race between the
  //             operator still holding the button, init() clearing the flag,
  //             and the active branch. Now latched locally in l_bEnding, which
  //             init() cannot reach, and both detector loops exit on it.
  //             Belt stops once and stays stopped. No motion parameters,
  //             speeds, or pick/place logic were changed.
  //  Modified : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - EARLY BELT ADVANCE rebased on top of
  //             the End Cycle fix above (previously these were two separate
  //             bundles from different bases and could not both be deployed).
  //             Sets bLastPart, arms the tkCheckZone lift watcher at gripper
  //             close, routes to the belt branch when the kick fired, and adds
  //             the belt-runaway cap. bEarlyBeltKick ships FALSE and
  //             nDetectorTries ships 0, so neither changes belt behaviour until
  //             switched on. Every new belt path is gated on l_bEnding and
  //             bStart so none of it can override an End Cycle stop.
  //  Modified : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - early-kick sequence tightened to:
  //             belt on at last-part lift -> rapid detect until a part appears
  //             -> stop belt -> delay(nPartsSettle) -> trigger trajectory.
  //             Suppresses the duplicate near-zero cycle-time sample on that
  //             path. The two pre-existing delay(0.5) settles in the detector
  //             branches are LEFT AT 0.5 on purpose - an earlier revision of
  //             mine pointed them at nPartsSettle (which ships 1.0) and so
  //             added half a second to every ordinary empty-scene pass, with
  //             or without the kick. nPartsSettle applies to tkBeltScan only.
  //  Modified : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - SEQUENCE FIX. The runaway-cap wrapper
  //             ("while nCountPlatesFound==0") could skip the detect loop
  //             entirely, because on the early-kick path nCountPlatesFound was
  //             seeded by AperaPartsFound() during the Place+Return move - a
  //             read taken with the belt running. A stale non-zero count meant
  //             ZERO detects: belt ran the whole place cycle then stopped with
  //             no detect-based stop. nCountPlatesFound is now zeroed before
  //             both loops so at least one fresh detect always runs.
  //  Modified : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - CYCLE INSTRUMENTATION, part 3.
  //             Three changes, all measurement or tunability, no motion:
  //               1. delay(0.35) after the gripper arms is now the public
  //                  nGripSettle, and tGripperEMH's single nEMH_gripperDelay is
  //                  now nEMH_onDelay / nEMH_offDelay. All three ship at the
  //                  values that were hard-coded, so deployment changes
  //                  nothing until they are swept. ~1.35 s of a 4.66 s cycle
  //                  is these three dwells and none of it is motion.
  //               2. SegPlaceReturn (46% of the cycle, one opaque number) is
  //                  split into SegCapture / SegDropClear / SegDescend /
  //                  SegMagnetOff / SegPartsFound / SegReturn, and
  //                  SegGripperActuate into SegMagnetOn / SegGripSettle.
  //                  SegPartsFound is the second, previously invisible vision
  //                  round-trip. Timestamps come from _nPlaceClk, recorded by
  //                  Part_Accept/Part_Reject; all arithmetic is here.
  //               3. SegRetreat FIXED. It read 0.00 on every cycle because it
  //                  timed a motion queue, not motion. Its end moved to the
  //                  waitEndMove that already existed in Part_Accept. No
  //                  waitEndMove was added anywhere - that would have broken
  //                  the retreat-to-drop blend.
  //             SCHEMA DRIFT: SegRetreat and SegPlaceReturn both changed
  //             meaning. Samples of those two keys from before 2026-09-03 are
  //             not comparable with later ones.
  //             Raw per-cycle samples are kept in _nSegLast and that is what
  //             LogCycleCSV logs - see the note where it is zeroed, and do not
  //             go back to reading _sCycleTime[] strings for the CSV.
  //  Modified : Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - PARALLEL BELT SCAN. Part_Accept and
  //             Part_Reject capture the next trajectory during the place move
  //             ("Take Apera Picture before dropping part"), and the early
  //             kick was running the belt straight through that capture - a
  //             moving-conveyor image, hence stale/invalid trajectories and
  //             the no-pickable-parts feed loop. tkBeltScan now scans for
  //             arriving plates in PARALLEL with the place motion and stops
  //             the belt on detection; Part_Accept/Reject capture only once it
  //             has ACKNOWLEDGED release of the APERA socket (bScanDone),
  //             otherwise the capture is deferred to jApera_Trigger_pos here.
  //             One unlocked APERA socket - see the header in tkBeltScan.pgx,
  //             which is the authority on the handshake. Three rules came out
  //             of reviewing this and are worth restating:
  //               * only bScanDone means "socket free". Clearing bScanForParts
  //                 does not - tkBeltScan may be inside apera_send when you
  //                 clear it, and calling apera_* then is the arm-crash case.
  //               * cancellation is a REQUEST (bScanCancel) that must be
  //                 acknowledged, never a unilateral seizure.
  //               * the wait is bounded on SILENCE (nScanHeartbeat), not on
  //                 total time, because a long wait here is legitimate - it is
  //                 the cell parked out of shot while the belt hunts for parts.
  //                 On silence we stop the belt and END, we do not grab the
  //                 socket.
  //             Note also that "wait" is not "unwait": apera_send consumes its
  //             own reply, so apera_wait_for_trajectory is a QUERY that may be
  //             repeated or skipped. Interleaving is the hazard, not pairing.
  //  Modified : Aug 2026 (Cowork/Claude) - replaced the old single 10-sample
  //             cycle-time bookkeeping with calls to UpdateCycleWindow() so
  //             the 10/25/50/100/1000-sample rolling windows all update from
  //             one place, and added per-segment timing (Approach, Linear
  //             Approach, Gripper Actuate, Retreat, Place+Return, Vision
  //             Wait) feeding the new "Cycle Timing" SP2 page. No motion
  //             parameters, speeds, or pick/place logic were changed.
  //  Oct 2026 v2.1.0 (Cowork/Claude): JOG ON A BLOCKED BEAM. Both detector
  //             loops spun forever when the beam was broken and APERA saw
  //             no plate (nDetectorTries ships 0 = unlimited): nNoPickOnBeam
  //             was only counted AFTER the loop, so the belt never jogged
  //             (19 Oct: 190 captures / 86 s and 57 / 28 s). Now ONE empty
  //             capture on a broken beam ends the loop and counts a miss;
  //             a capture that failed on a socket error is not a miss.
  //  Oct 2026 v2.2.0 (Cowork/Claude): STALE-TRAJECTORY GUARD before every pick
  //             - see the block above link(pPoseTransform,world).
  //  Oct 2026 v2.3.0 (Cowork/Claude): capture-while-moving (WaitMoveCapture),
  //             no stop at jApera_Trigger_pos when the picture is taken,
  //             no backtrack, AperaPartsFound only on a failed trajectory,
  //             mHover into jTrajectoryStart, optional APPROACH blend.
  //Header end
  //Initialize local variables
  l_bCycleDone=false
  l_bEnding=false
  l_bKickPath=false
  l_nNOTrajectoryFound=0
  //  0 means "never logged" for the beam-alarm reminder - see BeltAlarmHold.
  l_nAlarmClk=0
  //  Owned by this task; cleared here as well as by init() so that a task
  //  restart can never leave the conveyor held off by a stale in-flight flag.
  bPickInFlight=false
  //
  //Step 1
  //Enter pipeline group
  sPipelineGroup=""
  //
  //Step 2
  //++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++CTC 6/24++++7/19
  //  if dioGet(dDI14in_tieplate)==1
  //    l_sPipeline="14in_tieplate"
  //  elseIf dioGet(dDI18in_tieplate)==1
  //    l_sPipeline="18in_tieplate"
  //  endIf
  //+++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++added to override selecting on HMI CTC  6/24++++ 18in added 7/19
  //
  //Check if program stoped while rejecting part____CTC 12/11
  if bRejecting==true
    movej(appro(pReject_Station[1],{0,0,0,0,0,0}),tAR70,mSlow)
    waitEndMove()
  else
    l_pHere=here(tAR70,world)
    trAppro.z=-25
    movel(appro(l_pHere,trAppro),tAR70,mSlow)
  endIf
  //
  movej(jHome,tAR70,mSlow)
  movej(jApera_Trigger_pos,tAR70,mCycle)
  waitEndMove()
  //--- Own the APERA socket before the first capture (Sep 2026) ------------
  //  These three calls used to be unguarded, and this is the one place a task
  //  starts from completely unknown global state: monitoring recreates
  //  "cycle" whenever bStart goes true, which tkIOMonitor re-asserts from the
  //  physical start input. So a previous cycle could have ended with
  //  tkBeltScan still stuck inside apera_send, and this task would then write
  //  the socket underneath it. Every other apera site in this program is
  //  guarded; this was the gap.
  //  If the socket cannot be obtained we must not touch it at all, so this
  //  task ends - but it must end PROPERLY. Simply returning is not enough:
  //  bStart would still be true, monitoring would recreate "cycle" at once,
  //  and every retry re-runs the approach above - movel(appro(here,-25)),
  //  movej(jHome), movej(jApera_Trigger_pos) - before blocking for another
  //  nScanTimeout_s. That is the arm shuttling between home and the trigger
  //  pose every 30 s with only a log line to show for it.
  //  moveToHome() is the normal end-of-cycle path and ends with init(), which
  //  drops bStart, so monitoring leaves the task alone until the operator
  //  starts again. It makes no apera call of its own, and init()'s own
  //  handshake refuses to clearBuffer under a live scan.
  call AperaOwnSocket(l_bSocketFree)
  if l_bSocketFree==false
    //  BELT: not touched. tkBeltSensor owns bOUTPUTS["Output3"] and drops the
    //  conveyor on its own the moment bStart goes false, which moveToHome() ->
    //  init() does two statements below. Writing it here would be a second
    //  writer for no gain. See the single-writer rule in tkBeltSensor.pgx.
    logMsg("RAIV/T1: cycle task cannot obtain the APERA socket at start - homing and ending",1)
    wait(isEmpty())
    call moveToHome()
    return
  endIf
  //--- FIRST CAPTURE OF THE RUN, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) -----------------
  //  At this point the cell has just started, so the beam is very likely
  //  clear and the conveyor is ALREADY RUNNING - tkBeltSensor started it the
  //  moment bStart went true. Triggering here without waiting would plan the
  //  first pick of the run from a picture of a moving belt.
  //  So the first capture waits exactly like every later one: a plate at the
  //  pick end, the belt stopped and settled, and the arm out of the camera.
  //  The robot is at jApera_Trigger_pos here, having just been homed, so the
  //  FoV wait is expected to pass immediately.
  call WaitBeltParts(l_bBeltReady)
  call WaitFovClear(l_bFovClear)
  //Trigger trajectory
  //  v2.2.0: remember the belt state this picture is taken in.
  nTrajBeltStarts=nBeltMasterStarts
  bTrajBeltOk=bBeltReadyForCapture
  call apera:apera_trigger_trajectory(sPipeline,jTrajectoryStart,jTrajectoryEnd)
  //  APERA LATENCY, Sep 2026. Stamped after EVERY trigger site, so the wait
  //  can report how long APERA really took and not just how long the program
  //  blocked. See the long note at the wait.
  _nTrigClk=clock()
  //Parts Found
  call AperaPartsFound()
  //Wait for APERA to finish processing and return a trajectory
  call apera:apera_wait_for_trajectory(sPipeline,sPipelineGroup,bTrajectoryFound,pPose,pPoseTransform,nPickPointID)
  //
  while (bStart==true)
    //Step 3
    //Wait for APERA to finish processing and return a trajectory
    if l_bCycleDone==false
      //--- MEASURE ONLY, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) -----------------------------
      //  This block runs ONLY after Branch B ("No Pickable Parts Found"), which
      //  is the only place l_bCycleDone is left false. Branch B has ALREADY
      //  done its own apera_trigger_trajectory + apera_wait_for_trajectory a
      //  few statements earlier, and this waits AGAIN with no trigger in
      //  between. Whether that second wait returns instantly off a cached
      //  result or costs another full round trip is not something the log can
      //  currently say, and it is a ~0.28 s/pick question at run 19's Branch B
      //  rate (one pass every 4.7 picks). So: time it. Nothing else changes -
      //  no move added, none removed, no condition touched.
      //  The movej below only QUEUES, so this window is the wait, not motion.
      l_nLoopTopT0=clock()
      //++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++Replaced JStart with JAera Capture. Move JStart to if Trajectory found.
      //movej(jTrajectoryStart,tSchunkEMH084,mCycle)
      movej(jApera_Trigger_pos,tAR70,mCycle)
      //+++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++^^CTC 6/25
      call apera:apera_wait_for_trajectory(sPipeline,sPipelineGroup,bTrajectoryFound,pPose,pPoseTransform,nPickPointID)
      //++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++Added JStart here once trajectory is responded CTC 7/24
      movej(jTrajectoryStart,tAR70,mCycle)
      nLoopTopWait_s=clock()-l_nLoopTopT0
      nLoopTopCount=nLoopTopCount+1
    endIf
    //Create a point for the APERA offsets
    //--- v2.2.0 STALE-TRAJECTORY GUARD (Oct 2026, Cowork/Claude) -----------
    //  The belt is a hydraulic motor and coasts a load-dependent distance.
    //  A trajectory is only used if the belt was stopped and settled when the
    //  picture was triggered (bTrajBeltOk) AND has not started since
    //  (nBeltMasterStarts unchanged - every belt start, jog included, bumps it).
    //  Otherwise it is thrown away and a new picture is taken from
    //  jApera_Trigger_pos. This is NOT a miss: nNoPickOnBeam is not touched,
    //  so it never causes a jog.
    while bTrajectoryFound==true and (bTrajBeltOk==false or nBeltMasterStarts!=nTrajBeltStarts)
      if bStart==false or bEndCycleRobot==true or bAbort==true
        bTrajectoryFound=false
      else
        nTrajStaleDiscards=nTrajStaleDiscards+1
        logMsg("RAIV/T1: belt was moving at or after the trajectory picture - trajectory discarded, taking a new one (total "+toString("",nTrajStaleDiscards)+")",1)
        movej(jApera_Trigger_pos,tAR70,mCycle)
        waitEndMove()
        call WaitBeltParts(l_bBeltReady)
        call WaitFovClear(l_bFovClear)
        if l_bBeltReady==true and l_bFovClear==true
          nTrajBeltStarts=nBeltMasterStarts
          bTrajBeltOk=bBeltReadyForCapture
          call apera:apera_trigger_trajectory(sPipeline,jTrajectoryStart,jTrajectoryEnd)
          _nTrigClk=clock()
          call apera:apera_wait_for_trajectory(sPipeline,sPipelineGroup,bTrajectoryFound,pPose,pPoseTransform,nPickPointID)
          movej(jTrajectoryStart,tAR70,mCycle)
        else
          call BeltAlarmHold(l_nAlarmClk)
        endIf
      endIf
    endWhile
    link(pPoseTransform,world)
    // pPoseTransform=l_pPoseTransform
    nPickPoseID=nPickPointID
    //Check whether there was an error
    if bAperaError
      //******************** Add message for Robot Status *************************
    endIf
    //Step 4
    if bTrajectoryFound
      //+++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++inserted Jstart move to here CTC 6/25____Removed 7/24
      //movej(jTrajectoryStart,tSchunkEMH084,mCycle)
      //++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++CTC^^
      l_bCycleDone=false
      l_nNOTrajectoryFound=0
      //--- Early belt advance: is this the last part in the scene? -------------
      //  The bTrajectoryFound gate is NOT redundant - without it this reads true
      //  on a ZERO-part scene too, which on Raiv-Lab armed a kick for a pick
      //  that never happened and hung the cell. Keep both halves.
      //
      //  ***  THE THRESHOLD IS NOW TUNABLE, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude)  ***
      //  ***  IT SHIPS AT 1, WHICH IS THE NUMBER THAT WAS HARD-CODED  ***
      //
      //  WHY. This one comparison decides which of two belt-advance paths the
      //  cell takes, and they cost wildly different amounts:
      //    armed   -> tkCheckZone kicks the belt on the lift, tkBeltScan scans
      //               while the robot is still placing, early trigger fires.
      //               Overlapped with the pick. Costs ~nothing.
      //    not     -> the scene runs dry with no advance queued,
      //               apera_wait_for_trajectory returns nothing reachable, and
      //               the cell falls into Branch B: 3.36 s with the arm PARKED.
      //  Run 20, 203 picks: 63 belt advances, 26 armed and 34 through Branch B.
      //  That is +0.56 s on EVERY pick, decided here.
      //
      //  WHY THE COUNT DOES NOT REACH 1. The operator watched APERA count the
      //  plate IN THE GRIPPER, and the log agrees: nCountPlatesFound has read 0
      //  exactly three times in 511 picks while the belt genuinely empties
      //  every six, and turning Capture @ FoV on - which fires the capture with
      //  the arm still over the conveyor - lifts the mean count 2.89 -> 3.31
      //  and collapses the "count==1" bucket from 21% of picks to 10%.
      //  With a phantom plate in the count, an empty belt reads 1 and
      //  one-real-plate-left reads 2, so <=1 arms late or not at all.
      //  92% of early triggers fire on a pick where the count is <=1, against a
      //  6% baseline - this gate is doing nothing else.
      //
      //  THIS IS A WORKAROUND, AND IT SHOULD BE SAID OUT LOUD. The real fix is
      //  to mask the gripper out of the APERA pipeline so the count means what
      //  this line thinks it means. Raising the threshold buys the cycle time
      //  back without fixing the cause.
      //
      //  THE TRADE AT 2: the belt advances while ONE REAL PLATE is still in the
      //  field. tkBeltScan stops the belt as soon as it sees new plates, so
      //  that plate moves downstream rather than disappearing - but whether it
      //  is still pickable there is a question about THIS conveyor, which is
      //  why this ships at 1 and is turned up by somebody standing at the cell.
      //
      //  Clamped into a LOCAL, and clamped LOW AT 1 rather than 0. At 0 the
      //  test would be "count<=0 and a trajectory was found", i.e. it could
      //  only ever arm on the zero-part scene the comment above warns about -
      //  precisely the Raiv-Lab hang. The operator cannot type that in.
      //  The high clamp is 5: above that the kick arms on nearly every pick and
      //  the belt runs continuously, which is not a sweep, it is a fault.
      l_nLPThr=nLastPartThreshold
      if l_nLPThr<1
        l_nLPThr=1
      endIf
      if l_nLPThr>5
        l_nLPThr=5
      endIf
      //  Published so the log can show what the gate actually saw, which is the
      //  only way to tell "the threshold is too low" from "the scene really was
      //  full". Measurement: nothing reads either of these.
      nCountAtArm=nCountPlatesFound
      //--- LAST-PART DETECTION REMOVED, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) --------------
      //  Was: bLastPart=(bTrajectoryFound==true and nCountPlatesFound<=l_nLPThr)
      //  and it armed the early belt kick in tkCheckZone. Requested by Coty:
      //  the belt is no longer to be started from "this looked like the last
      //  part". The reason is measured - on 11 Sep the pick pipeline reported
      //  a mean of 4.12 plates against 2.98 that were actually pickable, and
      //  reported a non-zero count on 180 of 180 frames including two where
      //  the belt was provably empty, because it was counting the plate held
      //  on the magnet. Every belt decision taken from that count inherited
      //  the error.
      //  bLastPart is HELD FALSE rather than deleted: it is still bound to the
      //  Options page and to the CSV, and a global that quietly stops being
      //  written would read as "never the last part" without saying why.
      //  nLastPartThreshold and nCountAtArm are likewise kept and still
      //  published, so the old series stays readable; nothing reads them.
      bLastPart=false
      //  Cleared per pick; Part_Accept/Reject or the deferred block sets it.
      bTrajTriggered=false
      //--- Per-cycle raw segment samples, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) ------------
      //  _nSegLast holds THIS pick's raw seconds for every segment, and it is
      //  what LogCycleCSV logs. It exists because reading the numbers back out
      //  of _sCycleTime[] - which is what the CSV used to do - is wrong for a
      //  segment that can legitimately be ZERO:
      //    * UpdateCycleWindow writes LastSec only when the sample is non-zero
      //      (a deliberate guard against a bogus 0-second cycle), so a segment
      //      that measured 0 this pick logs the PREVIOUS pick's number, and
      //      one that has NEVER measured logs an EMPTY STRING - which puts a
      //      ",," in the CSV and mis-aligns every column after it.
      //    * SegDropClear is exactly 0 whenever the drop station was already
      //      clear, which is the NORMAL case. SegCapture is ~0 on the early
      //      path. So this is not a corner case, it is most cycles.
      //  Zeroed here, at the top of the pick, so nothing can be stale: a
      //  segment that took no sample this pick logs a true 0.
      //  INDEX MAP - LogCycleCSV depends on it, keep them in step:
      //    0 cycle          5 place+return    10 magnet off
      //    1 approach       6 vision wait     11 parts found
      //    2 lin approach   7 capture         12 return
      //    3 gripper total  8 drop clear      13 magnet on
      //    4 retreat        9 descend         14 grip settle
      for l_nSegI=0 to size(_nSegLast)-1
        _nSegLast[l_nSegI]=0
      endFor
      //  Also cleared per pick - tkCheckZone sets it when the kick fires
      //  during this pick's retreat, and it is read later in this same pass.
      bEarlyKickFired=false
      //--- Which BELT BATCH does this pick belong to? Sep 2026 -----------------
      //  One belt advance delivers one batch of plates; tkBeltSensor counts
      //  those advances into nBeltBatchNo. Stamped HERE, and here for a
      //  reason: apera_wait_for_trajectory has just returned, so THIS is the
      //  moment this pick's plate was chosen out of what the belt had
      //  delivered. That makes the attribution unambiguous, and it has to be,
      //  because the belt can advance DURING a pick - that is exactly what the
      //  early kick is for. Stamping at the end of the pick instead would put
      //  the last pick of every batch into the next batch's group whenever the
      //  kick fired, which is most picks with bEarlyBeltKick on.
      //  LogCycleCSV reads this to size the batch and logs it as line H, and
      //  the parser groups rows on it. A batch that gets NO pick simply leaves
      //  a gap in the sequence, and that gap is itself the useful number: a
      //  belt advance that delivered nothing pickable.
      //  ONE assignment. Read of another task's num, write of a private num.
      //  No motion, no IO, no branch, nothing behavioural depends on it.
      _nBatchAtPick=nBeltBatchNo
      //-------------------------------------------------------------------------
      //--- PICK IN FLIGHT, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) ---------------------------
      //  From here until the retreat is issued, APERA's planned grip point must
      //  not move. tkBeltSensor reads this flag and will not start the conveyor
      //  while it is true, even if the beam is clear.
      //  That is the ONE thing allowed to defer "beam clear -> belt runs", and
      //  it defers it by at most one pick. Without it, a scene whose remaining
      //  plates are all UPSTREAM of the beam reads as "beam clear" and the belt
      //  would slide the target out from under a committed trajectory - the
      //  Raiv-Lab over-torque failure.
      //  tkCycle is the only writer. It is cleared on every exit from the pick
      //  below, including the ending path, so it cannot latch.
      bPickInFlight=true
      //Execute APERA's AUTOPILOT for Pick a Tie Plate from Conveyor
      //--- Per-segment timing: Approach ---------------------------------------
      l_nSegT0=clock()
      //  v2.3.0: with bBlendApproach the arm no longer stops dead above the
      //  plate - APPROACH blends into LINEAR_APPROACH within nApproachBlend_mm.
      //  The waitEndMove()+isSettled() before the grip are unchanged.
      if bBlendApproach==true
        mApproachBlend.leave=nApproachBlend_mm
        mApproachBlend.reach=nApproachBlend_mm
        call apera:apera_follow_trajectory("APPROACH",tAR70,mApproachBlend)
      else
        call apera:apera_follow_trajectory("APPROACH",tAR70,mCycle)
        //Turn on AirSteream to clear pick point of dibris________________________CTC 12/13
        waitEndMove()
      endIf
      _nSegLast[1]=clock()-l_nSegT0
      call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegApproach,_nSegApproachIndex,_nSegLast[1],"SegApproach",false)
      //-------------------------------------------------------------------------
      dioSet(dDOAirStream,1)
      //dioSet(dDOemh_gripperON,1)
      //call tGripperEMH("ON")
      //--- Per-segment timing: Linear Approach + settle -----------------------
      l_nSegT0=clock()
      call apera:apera_follow_trajectory("LINEAR_APPROACH",tAR70,mLinearMove)
      waitEndMove()
      wait(isSettled())
      _nSegLast[2]=clock()-l_nSegT0
      call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegLinearApproach,_nSegLinearApproachIndex,_nSegLast[2],"SegLinearApproach",false)
      //-------------------------------------------------------------------------
      dioSet(dDOAirStream,0)
      //____________________________
      call PartPicked()
      //call tMagnet("ON")__________________________________CTC 11/20  Schunk Gripper
      //--- Per-segment timing: Gripper Actuate ---------------------------------
      //  SPLIT Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) into two sub-segments so the two
      //  dwells inside it can be swept independently:
      //      SegMagnetOn   - queue drain + the GRIP pulse, delay(nEMH_onDelay)
      //      SegGripSettle - the lift-watcher arming + delay(nGripSettle)
      //  SegGripperActuate is unchanged in meaning - it still spans T0 to the
      //  same instant it always did - and the two halves sum to it EXACTLY,
      //  because all three are differences of the same three stamps and no
      //  UpdateCycleWindow call sits between them. That identity is worth
      //  keeping: it is the cheapest check that the split is honest.
      //  The boundary is the RETURN of tGripperEMH("ON"), i.e. after the grip
      //  pulse - not its internal waitEndMove. Nothing was added to the motion
      //  path; tkCycle already did waitEndMove() + wait(isSettled()) two
      //  statements earlier, so the queue is drained before T0 and SegMagnetOn
      //  is the pulse alone.
      //  Together these two are ~0.85 s of a 4.66 s cycle, every cycle, to the
      //  centisecond: pure dwell with no motion in it.
      l_nSegT0=clock()
      call tGripperEMH("ON")
      l_nSegT1=clock()
      //--- Early belt advance: arm the lift watcher (tkCheckZone) --------------
      //  Gripper just closed, so the TCP is at the REAL height this part was
      //  gripped from - the datum the 3 in lift is measured against. Arming here
      //  is also the only thing that lets the watcher fire, so a kick is
      //  structurally impossible without a part in the gripper.
      l_pHere=here(tAR70,world)
      nBeltPickZ_mm=l_pHere.trsf.z
      //  nTCP_beltLift_mm=0 removed Sep 2026: tkBeltSensor recomputes it from
      //  nBeltPickZ_mm every poll and is now its sole writer. Zeroing it here
      //  as well is a benign race today and a confusing one to debug later.
      nBeltArmClk=clock()
      bBeltLiftArmed=true
      //-------------------------------------------------------------------------
      //  Was a hard-coded delay(0.35). Made public as nGripSettle Sep 2026 so
      //  it can be swept from the data editor without a reload. Ships at 0.35,
      //  which is exactly what was hard-coded, so this is behaviour-neutral
      //  until the number is changed. This one is about the PLATE settling on
      //  the magnet, not the coil - judge it by watching the first centimetres
      //  of the retreat for the plate shifting, not by the pick succeeding.
      delay(nGripSettle)
      //  One stamp, three differences - so SegMagnetOn + SegGripSettle equals
      //  SegGripperActuate to the last decimal place. Taking clock() again per
      //  publish would let the publishes themselves leak into the numbers and
      //  the identity would drift.
      l_nSegT2=clock()
      _nSegLast[13]=l_nSegT1-l_nSegT0
      _nSegLast[14]=l_nSegT2-l_nSegT1
      _nSegLast[3]=l_nSegT2-l_nSegT0
      call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegMagnetOn,_nSegMagnetOnIndex,_nSegLast[13],"SegMagnetOn",false)
      call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegGripSettle,_nSegGripSettleIndex,_nSegLast[14],"SegGripSettle",false)
      call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegGripperActuate,_nSegGripperActuateIndex,_nSegLast[3],"SegGripperActuate",false)
      //--- DID THE MAGNET ACTUALLY TAKE THE PLATE?  Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) --
      //  Two dioGets, no side effect, nothing branches on them. Sampled HERE:
      //  the ON pulse and the settle have both elapsed and the retreat has not
      //  started, so this is the last instant at which "the grip either worked
      //  or it did not" is a clean question.
      //    nGripPartAtPick  expected 1. A 0 is a FAILED GRIP - nEMH_onDelay or
      //                     nGripSettle too short, or no plate there at all.
      //    nGripMagAtPick   expected 1. It separates "the coil never energised"
      //                     from "the coil energised and the plate did not
      //                     come" - different faults, different fixes, and
      //                     indistinguishable without it.
      //  Both inputs are wired (Term_2 %I6 and %I4) and were read by NOTHING
      //  in this project except one lamp on the Options page. The 2026-09-09
      //  dwell sweep ran 295 picks at three settings and could not say whether
      //  a single plate was gripped; that is what these two close.
      //  DELIBERATELY NOT A GATE. Nothing waits on them and nothing aborts.
      //  Making the cycle depend on a sensor that has never been exercised
      //  would be a behaviour change smuggled in behind a measurement.
      nGripPartAtPick=dioGet(dDIemh_Gripper_PartAvailable)
      nGripMagAtPick=dioGet(dDIemh_Gripper_MagnetizationStatus)
      //-------------------------------------------------------------------------
      //--- Per-segment timing: Retreat ------------------------------------------
      //  ***  FIXED Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude)  ***
      //  This used to stamp the clock immediately after queueing the two
      //  apera_follow_trajectory calls below. movej/movel only QUEUE motion
      //  and return, so it measured QUEUE TIME, not motion: it read 0.00 on
      //  all 25 samples of the window while the retreat it names clearly takes
      //  time. Anyone reading the Cycle Timing page concluded the retreat was
      //  free. It was not - it was unmeasured, and it was being counted inside
      //  SegPlaceReturn instead.
      //
      //  It is now closed inside Part_Accept/Part_Reject, at the waitEndMove()
      //  that is ALREADY THERE with the arm at rest 50 mm above the drop. That
      //  waitEndMove drains LINEAR_RETREAT, RETREAT and the drop-approach movej
      //  together, so SegRetreat now means "retreat off the belt plus the
      //  approach to the drop, arm at rest".
      //
      //  NOTHING WAS ADDED TO THE MOTION PATH. The obvious fix - a
      //  waitEndMove() after RETREAT here - would stop the arm at the retreat
      //  end point and break its blend into the drop approach. That is a
      //  behaviour change, it would cost time, and it would add exactly the
      //  jerk we are trying to remove. The measurement boundary moved; the
      //  motion did not.
      //
      //  SCHEMA DRIFT - SegRetreat and SegPlaceReturn BOTH change meaning
      //  here. Pre-2026-09-03 samples of those two keys are not comparable
      //  with later ones. SegRetreat goes 0.00 -> a real number; SegPlaceReturn
      //  loses the retreat and drop-approach motion it used to hide.
      //
      //  _nPlaceClk[0] is zeroed here as the staleness sentinel: if the place
      //  program does not reach its waitEndMove, the publish block below finds
      //  0 and falls back to the old whole-window measurement rather than
      //  computing sub-segments from last cycle's timestamps.
      _nRetreatT0=clock()
      _nPlaceClk[0]=0
      call apera:apera_follow_trajectory("LINEAR_RETREAT",tAR70,mLinearVariable)
      call apera:apera_follow_trajectory("RETREAT",tAR70,mCycleVariable)
      //--- PICK NO LONGER IN FLIGHT ------------------------------------------
      //  The plate is on the magnet and the retreat is issued, so belt motion
      //  can no longer disturb this pick. Released HERE rather than after the
      //  place, so the conveyor gets its head start across the whole place and
      //  return move - which is where the 11 Sep measurement showed the early
      //  belt kick was earning its 2.6-2.9 s. Same overlap, decided by the
      //  beam instead of by a plate count.
      //  A successful pick also clears the not-pickable counter, which returns
      //  the belt master's nudge budget.
      bPickInFlight=false
      nNoPickOnBeam=0
      //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
      //
      //--- Per-segment timing: Place Part + return toward vision pose ----------
      //  l_nSegT0 here is only the FALLBACK anchor - see the publish block at
      //  the end of this window. On the normal path the window is measured
      //  from _nPlaceClk[0] instead, so that the retreat and drop-approach
      //  motion belongs to SegRetreat and is not double-counted here.
      l_nSegT0=clock()
      if sTiePlateApera==sTiePlateUser
        call Part_Accept()
      else
        call Part_Reject()
//        call Part_Accept()

      endIf


      //--- Deferred trajectory capture (Sep 2026, Cowork/Claude) --------------
      //  Part_Accept/Part_Reject capture during the place move whenever the
      //  socket is free (the fast path, original timing - and note there is
      //  deliberately NO FOV term in that gate; see the long note in
      //  Part_Accept). If it was not - tkBeltScan was still hunting arriving plates
      //  - the part has now been DROPPED, so this is where the image is taken:
      //  park out of the camera FOV at jApera_Trigger_pos and wait for parts.
      //
      //  bScanDone is the release flag, and it is the right thing to wait on:
      //  tkBeltScan publishes it LAST on every exit path (plates found, stop,
      //  end cycle), after it has switched the belt OFF and served the
      //  nPartsSettle delay. One test therefore means belt stopped AND parts
      //  settled AND socket free.
      //
      //  The waiting itself lives in AperaOwnSocket() - one program, called
      //  from every site in this project that needs the socket, so there is a
      //  single copy of that logic. Read its header; the short version is that
      //  it waits for an ACKNOWLEDGEMENT (bScanDone), bounds SILENCE rather
      //  than total wait (a long wait here is often correct - it is the cell
      //  parked out of shot while the belt hunts for parts), and on failure
      //  returns false meaning "make no apera call at all".
      //  HANDSHAKE FIRST, ON EVERY PATH - this ordering matters twice over.
      //  It used to sit inside the "if bTrajTriggered==false" below, which
      //  created two defects the moment tkBeltScan gained its own early
      //  trigger:
      //    * bTrajTriggered was tested BEFORE the scan could set it, so if
      //      tkCycle got here first it entered the block, waited out the scan
      //      inside AperaOwnSocket - by which time the early trigger HAD
      //      fired - and then issued a SECOND trigger for the same pick. Not
      //      a socket hazard (the query is idempotent) but it threw away the
      //      whole benefit and still made the park move. Both orderings occur
      //      routinely: tkCycle's path here is the place move plus the
      //      dDIDropClear wait, tkBeltScan's is plate travel plus nPartsSettle.
      //    * on the early path the handshake was skipped entirely, so the
      //      AperaPartsFound gate below could find bScanDone still false and
      //      silently skip a call the original made unconditionally, leaving
      //      nCountPlatesFound holding the PREVIOUS pick's count - which then
      //      fed bLastPart and the branch routing.
      //  Running it here fixes both: by the time bTrajTriggered is tested it
      //  is final, and bScanDone is confirmed on every path.
      //  Cost when there is nothing to wait for (no kick, or the scan already
      //  finished): AperaOwnSocket runs one loop pass with no delay() and
      //  returns. Nothing measurable.
      call AperaOwnSocket(l_bSocketFree)
      if l_bSocketFree==false
        //  AperaOwnSocket has already dropped the belt and logged. It has told
        //  us the socket may be unusable, so we make NO apera call: latch the
        //  end and let the branches below home the robot.
        l_bEnding=true
      endIf
      //--- v2.3.0 POST-PLACE: NO DEAD STOPS, NO BACKTRACK (Oct 2026, Cowork/Claude)
      //  Was: full stop at jApera_Trigger_pos, a one-shot capture check, a
      //  blocking AperaPartsFound() (~0.5 s), a blend-OFF move to
      //  jTrajectoryStart (mReach), and - if the belt had not settled at that
      //  one check - a trip BACK to jApera_Trigger_pos to wait and capture.
      //  Now: jApera_Trigger_pos stays the via point (same route), but the arm
      //  only stops there if the picture still has not been taken when it
      //  arrives. WaitMoveCapture() takes it the moment the belt is ready.
      //  jTrajectoryStart is reached with mHover (small blend) so the arm flows
      //  into APPROACH when APERA has already answered. AperaPartsFound() now
      //  runs only when APERA returns NO trajectory - see after the wait below.
      if bTrajTriggered==true
        movej(jApera_Trigger_pos,tAR70,mCycle)
      else
        movej(jApera_Trigger_pos,tAR70,mCycle)
        call WaitMoveCapture()
        if bTrajTriggered==false and l_bSocketFree==true and l_bEnding==false and bEndCycleRobot==false and bStart==true and bAbort==false
          waitEndMove()
          if _bStartFoV==true
            nFovHitStart=nFovHitStart+1
          endIf
          if _bEndFoV==true
            nFovHitEnd=nFovHitEnd+1
          endIf
          call WaitBeltParts(l_bBeltReady)
          call WaitFovClear(l_bFovClear)
          if l_bBeltReady==true and l_bFovClear==true
            nTrajBeltStarts=nBeltMasterStarts
            bTrajBeltOk=bBeltReadyForCapture
            call apera:apera_trigger_trajectory(sPipeline,jTrajectoryStart,jTrajectoryEnd)
            _nTrigClk=clock()
            bTrajTriggered=true
            nCapAtTrigPos=nCapAtTrigPos+1
          endIf
        endIf
      endIf
      l_nSegPF=0
      _nSegLast[11]=0
      movej(jTrajectoryStart,tAR70,mHover)
      //--- Publish the retreat + place windows and their sub-segments ----------
      //  ***  THE PLACE+RETURN SPLIT, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude)  ***
      //  SegPlaceReturn was 2.13 s of a 4.66 s cycle - 46% - as ONE opaque
      //  number covering eight different things. This breaks it up. The split
      //  is measurement only: no motion, no dwell and no IO order changed.
      //  BE PRECISE ABOUT THE BOUNDARIES, because the whole reason SegRetreat
      //  was wrong is that someone was not:
      //    * [0], [2] and [3] land on real synchronisation points that already
      //      existed - Part_Accept's waitEndMove, its interlock wait(), and
      //      tGripperEMH's waitEndMove. Those three bound MOTION honestly.
      //    * _nRetreatT0 is a bare clock() taken just before two queue-only
      //      calls. That is correct as a START (nothing is in flight yet -
      //      tkCycle waited for isSettled two statements earlier) but it is
      //      not a sync point.
      //    * l_nSegT2 is taken AFTER movej(jTrajectoryStart) was merely
      //      QUEUED. So SegReturn and the tail of SegPlaceReturn end on a
      //      queue, not on motion. That is deliberate - the alternative is a
      //      waitEndMove that would change the motion - but it is exactly why
      //      SegReturn must not be read as motion time. See its note below.
      //
      //  Part_Accept/Part_Reject record five clock readings into _nPlaceClk and
      //  contain no logic of their own - see the note there. The boundaries:
      //    _nRetreatT0  -> LINEAR_RETREAT queued
      //    _nPlaceClk[0] -> arm AT REST 50 mm above the drop (their waitEndMove)
      //    _nPlaceClk[1] -> the trajectory-capture gate has been through
      //    _nPlaceClk[2] -> dDIDropClear / dDIPowerEnable interlock satisfied
      //    _nPlaceClk[3] -> descent motion drained (tGripperEMH's waitEndMove,
      //                     read back through _nGripSyncClk)
      //    _nPlaceClk[4] -> release pulse finished
      //  and therefore:
      //    SegRetreat    = retreat off the belt + approach to the drop
      //    SegCapture    = the APERA trigger round-trip, or ~0 when the belt
      //                    scan already captured early
      //    SegDropClear  = time waiting on the drop-station interlock
      //    SegDescend    = the 50 mm movel down, motion only
      //    SegMagnetOff  = the RELEASE dwell only - the third of the three
      //                    tunable dwells, and the one that was hidden here
      //    SegPartsFound = the second vision round-trip (published above)
      //    SegReturn     = the remainder: the retract queue, the deferred
      //                    capture block when it runs, and the final movej
      //                    queue. Mostly queue time, so expect it small and do
      //                    not read it as motion - that is the mistake
      //                    SegRetreat used to make.
      //                    ONE EXCEPTION worth knowing before reading a big
      //                    number here: AperaOwnSocket also runs inside this
      //                    span, and on the deferred path it can legitimately
      //                    block for up to nScanTimeout_s (30 s) while the
      //                    belt hunts for parts. A large SegReturn on a
      //                    last-part cycle is the cell parked out of shot
      //                    waiting for the scan, not a slow robot. Cross-check
      //                    it against nCapDeferred and SegCapture.
      //  SegRetreat + SegPlaceReturn cover the same span as the old pair did,
      //  so the cycle arithmetic on the Cycle Timing page is unaffected - to
      //  within the runtime of the one UpdateCycleWindow call that used to sit
      //  in the gap between the two windows and now does not. A few
      //  milliseconds; not exact, and not worth pretending it is.
      //
      //  l_nSegT2 is taken ONCE so every sub-segment is differenced against
      //  the same instant - two separate clock() calls here would let the
      //  parts drift out of sum.
      l_nSegT2=clock()
      if _nPlaceClk[0]!=0
        //  Raw samples first, from ONE reading of the clock, so the six parts
        //  add up to the parent exactly. _nSegLast is what LogCycleCSV logs;
        //  the UpdateCycleWindow calls below drive the pendant display.
        _nSegLast[4]=_nPlaceClk[0]-_nRetreatT0
        _nSegLast[7]=_nPlaceClk[1]-_nPlaceClk[0]
        _nSegLast[8]=_nPlaceClk[2]-_nPlaceClk[1]
        _nSegLast[9]=_nPlaceClk[3]-_nPlaceClk[2]
        _nSegLast[10]=_nPlaceClk[4]-_nPlaceClk[3]
        _nSegLast[12]=l_nSegT2-_nPlaceClk[4]-l_nSegPF
        _nSegLast[5]=l_nSegT2-_nPlaceClk[0]
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegRetreat,_nSegRetreatIndex,_nSegLast[4],"SegRetreat",false)
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegCapture,_nSegCaptureIndex,_nSegLast[7],"SegCapture",false)
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegDropClear,_nSegDropClearIndex,_nSegLast[8],"SegDropClear",false)
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegDescend,_nSegDescendIndex,_nSegLast[9],"SegDescend",false)
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegMagnetOff,_nSegMagnetOffIndex,_nSegLast[10],"SegMagnetOff",false)
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegReturn,_nSegReturnIndex,_nSegLast[12],"SegReturn",false)
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegPartsFound,_nSegPartsFoundIndex,_nSegLast[11],"SegPartsFound",false)
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegPlaceReturn,_nSegPlaceReturnIndex,_nSegLast[5],"SegPlaceReturn",false)
      else
        //  Fallback: the place program did not reach its waitEndMove, so there
        //  are no honest boundaries this pass. Publish the whole window the way
        //  the pre-split code did, and publish NOTHING for any sub-segment -
        //  SegRetreat and SegPartsFound included, which is why SegPartsFound is
        //  only STORED above and published inside the branch. A wrong number is
        //  never banked; _nSegLast was zeroed at the top of the pick, so the
        //  CSV logs true zeros for the sub-segments on such a pass.
        //  NOTE this path is not reachable today: both arms call Part_Accept
        //  and it has no early return, so _nPlaceClk[0] is always set by the
        //  time we get here. It exists so that a future third place path
        //  cannot silently poison the 25-slot rings with samples of the
        //  pre-split meaning mixed in with the new one.
        _nSegLast[5]=l_nSegT2-l_nSegT0
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegPlaceReturn,_nSegPlaceReturnIndex,_nSegLast[5],"SegPlaceReturn",false)
      endIf
      //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
      //++++++++Test moving "wait for trajectory" sooner. so not to pause at point...CTC 11/27 NOPE
      //--- Per-segment timing: Vision Wait for next trajectory ------------------
      //  SKIPPED on the early-kick path. This prefetch waits on a trajectory
      //  computed from an image taken BEFORE the belt started moving, so once
      //  the kick has fired that answer is already stale - the part it
      //  describes has been carried down the belt. We would block waiting for
      //  it, bank the wait as SegVisionWait, then throw it away and re-trigger
      //  from the detect branch anyway. Skip it and go straight to
      //  detect -> stop belt -> settle -> capture.
      //  Reads the result of the trigger issued by Part_Accept/Part_Reject or
      //  by the deferred block above.
      //
      //  HOW THIS ACTUALLY WORKS - worth having written down, because I got it
      //  wrong once in these comments. apera/functions/apera_send.pgx is:
      //      sAperaConn=x_sInput      // write the request
      //      x_sReturn=sAperaConn     // read ITS OWN reply
      //  So every apera_* routine is a self-contained request/response, and
      //  apera_wait_for_trajectory is a QUERY ("l,w.<pipeline>.motion_planner")
      //  not a queue pop. Trigger and wait are therefore NOT a one-for-one
      //  pair: the original code has always issued ONE trigger before the main
      //  loop and then queried TWICE (once immediately, once at the top of the
      //  first loop pass), which is why that works. Deliberately not quoting
      //  line numbers - they moved once already.
      //  Skipping or repeating this query cannot offset the socket.
      //
      //  What it CAN do is return a stale answer, and it costs a round-trip.
      //  bTrajTriggered is false only when the deferred block deliberately
      //  refused to trigger on End Cycle / stop, so querying then would ask
      //  the planner about a pick that was never requested. Skip it;
      //  bTrajectoryFound keeps its previous value, which is harmless because
      //  every branch below latches l_bEnding and homes.
      //
      //  The REAL socket hazard is not pairing, it is interleaving: two tasks
      //  inside apera_send at once means one reads the other's reply, because
      //  there is no lock between that write and that read. That is what the
      //  bScanForParts / bScanDone ownership handshake exists to prevent.
      //  nVisionWaitValid tells the CSV whether this cycle's SegVisionWait
      //  number is real. When the query is skipped the segment is not
      //  measured, so _sCycleTime["SegVisionWaitLastSec"] still holds the
      //  PREVIOUS cycle's value - which would otherwise be logged as if it
      //  were this cycle's and quietly bias the analysis.
      nVisionWaitValid=0
      //=====================================================================
      //  GUARANTEED CAPTURE, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude).  READ THIS.
      //=====================================================================
      //  Sensor-only belt control broke an assumption this code rested on.
      //  Before the change, Part_Accept captured during the place move on
      //  essentially every pick, so bTrajTriggered was true here and the query
      //  below returned a fresh answer. bTrajTriggered==false meant "End Cycle
      //  landed", and the comment above is right that the stale
      //  bTrajectoryFound was harmless in that case because every branch below
      //  latches l_bEnding and homes.
      //
      //  THAT IS NO LONGER TRUE. Part_Accept now refuses to capture while the
      //  conveyor is feeding, and the conveyor is feeding for most of the
      //  place move - that is the whole design. So bTrajTriggered arrives here
      //  false on ordinary picks, with nothing ending. Without this block the
      //  query is skipped, bTrajectoryFound keeps LAST pick's value, and the
      //  arm executes a trajectory planned for a plate that has since been
      //  picked and carried away. That is an arm-crash mode, not a slow cycle.
      //
      //  So: if nothing captured, capture here, and do it the way the cell was
      //  asked to. Move to the taught out-of-shot pose, wait for the beam to
      //  stop the belt, prove the arm is clear of the camera, then shoot.
      //  jApera_Trigger_pos is used rather than wherever the arm happens to be
      //  because it is the pose that was taught for exactly this and it is the
      //  one pose known to satisfy _bStartFoV==false.
      if bTrajTriggered==false and l_bEnding==false and bEndCycleRobot==false and bStart==true and bAbort==false
        movej(jApera_Trigger_pos,tAR70,mCycle)
        waitEndMove()
        call WaitBeltParts(l_bBeltReady)
        call WaitFovClear(l_bFovClear)
        if l_bBeltReady==true and l_bFovClear==true
          //  v2.2.0: remember the belt state this picture is taken in.
          nTrajBeltStarts=nBeltMasterStarts
          bTrajBeltOk=bBeltReadyForCapture
          call apera:apera_trigger_trajectory(sPipeline,jTrajectoryStart,jTrajectoryEnd)
          _nTrigClk=clock()
          bTrajTriggered=true
          nCapAtTrigPos=nCapAtTrigPos+1
        endIf
      endIf
      //  BELT AND BRACES. If we still have not triggered - stood down, alarmed,
      //  or the arm could not clear the camera - the query below is skipped and
      //  bTrajectoryFound must NOT be allowed to carry the previous pick's
      //  answer into the branch that moves the arm. Forcing it false routes the
      //  cycle into the "no pickable parts" branch, which waits for the belt
      //  and re-captures. Slow is the correct failure here; stale is not.
      if bTrajTriggered==false
        bTrajectoryFound=false
      endIf
      if bTrajTriggered==true
        l_nSegT0=clock()
        call apera:apera_wait_for_trajectory(sPipeline,l_sPipelineGroup,bTrajectoryFound,pPose,pPoseTransform,nPickPointID)
        _nSegLast[6]=clock()-l_nSegT0
        //--- HOW LONG DID APERA REALLY TAKE?  Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) -------
        //  _nSegLast[6] above is how long the PROGRAM BLOCKED here. It is NOT
        //  APERA's response time: the trigger fired earlier in the pick and
        //  APERA was working while the arm moved. nAperaLatency_s measures
        //  trigger -> answer, and the PAIR separates two very different worlds:
        //      latency ~= wait  -> the trigger is too LATE. Nothing is hidden
        //                          under motion, and moving the trigger earlier
        //                          in the pick recovers the wait.
        //      latency >> wait  -> most of it is ALREADY hidden and what is
        //                          left is APERA's own floor. Moving the
        //                          trigger buys nothing; the fix is vision-side.
        //  This is what decides whether the arm's pause at jTrajectoryStart -
        //  0.88 s, 20% of the cycle, on every one of 295 picks in the 09-09 run
        //  - is recoverable at all. Without it both answers look identical.
        //  Guarded: _nTrigClk is 0 until the first trigger of a run, and a
        //  latency measured from 0 would be the controller uptime.
        if _nTrigClk!=0
          nAperaLatency_s=clock()-_nTrigClk
        else
          nAperaLatency_s=0
        endIf
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nSegVisionWait,_nSegVisionWaitIndex,_nSegLast[6],"SegVisionWait",false)
        nVisionWaitValid=1
      endIf
      //--- v2.3.0: plate count only when it is needed -----------------------
      //  nCountPlatesFound decides below whether the cell carries on or goes
      //  to the belt-scan branch. A trajectory means at least one plate, so
      //  the ~0.5 s AperaPartsFound() round trip is only paid when APERA
      //  returned no trajectory.
      if bTrajectoryFound==true
        if nCountPlatesFound==0
          nCountPlatesFound=1
        endIf
      elseIf bTrajTriggered==true
        l_nSegT1=clock()
        if bScanDone==true and bScanForParts==false and l_bEnding==false
          call AperaPartsFound()
        endIf
        _nSegLast[11]=clock()-l_nSegT1
      else
        nCountPlatesFound=0
      endIf
      //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
      l_bCycleDone=true
      //--- Cycle Time: update the 10/25/50/100/1000-sample rolling windows -----
      if _nTimer!=0
        l_nElapsed=clock()-_nTimer
        _nSegLast[0]=l_nElapsed
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nCycleTime,_nCycleTimeIndex,l_nElapsed,"",true)
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nCycleTime25,_nCycleTimeIndex25,l_nElapsed,"25",true)
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nCycleTime50,_nCycleTimeIndex50,l_nElapsed,"50",true)
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nCycleTime100,_nCycleTimeIndex100,l_nElapsed,"100",true)
        call UpdateCycleWindow(_nCycleTime1000,_nCycleTimeIndex1000,l_nElapsed,"1000",true)
        //  Emit the cycle's metrics as CSV into the controller log. This is the
        //  PER-CYCLE TIME SERIES; it is what shows a trend.
        //  CORRECTION to what this comment used to say: globals DO reach a
        //  backup, but only if the application data is SAVED FROM SP2 before
        //  the backup is pulled. That was done once, before the 17h43m backup,
        //  and every counter came out with a live value. What the .dtx gives
        //  you is a point-in-time SNAPSHOT (free, no code); what the log gives
        //  you is the series. Both are worth having. Note the cost of that
        //  save: it strips every .dtx comment. Documentation belongs here in
        //  the .pgx files. See LogCycleCSV.
        //  Inside the "if _nTimer!=0" guard on purpose - no line is emitted
        //  for the first pass after a start, which has no valid cycle time.
        call LogCycleCSV()
      endIf
      //
      _nTimer=clock()
      //
      //Part has been picked up now check conveyor has parts
      //  Also skipped once the kick has fired: this inspects a scene the belt
      //  is actively moving, and nothing recomputes nCountPlatesFound from it
      //  before the branch below, so on that path it is a vision round-trip
      //  spent on a stale image. Unchanged off the kick path.
      //  l_bEnding is in the test to ENFORCE AperaOwnSocket's contract ("on
      //  false, make no apera call") rather than merely argue it. The argument
      //  that it is unreachable does hold today - every path that makes
      //  AperaOwnSocket fail above requires a kick to have fired, which sets
      //  bEarlyKickFired first - but that is a chain of three facts, and this
      //  is one term.
      //--- SENSOR-ONLY BELT: gated, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) ------------------
      //  bBeltReadyForCapture added. This inspect runs right after the place
      //  move, which is now exactly when the conveyor is feeding - so without
      //  the gate it is a full vision round-trip (~0.4 s) spent photographing a
      //  moving belt, every pick, for an answer nothing can use.
      //  It is kept rather than deleted because it writes nClassesFound /
      //  sClassID / nPartsDet, which the routing below and the HMI read, and
      //  because AperaPartsFound() - which DOES set nCountPlatesFound - runs
      //  separately. Gating it changes when it runs, not what it means.
      //  bEarlyKickFired is now permanently false (the kick is gone), so that
      //  term is inert and is left only so this line still reads as the
      //  original did if the kick is ever revived.
      if bEarlyKickFired==false and l_bEnding==false and bBeltReadyForCapture==true and _bStartFoV==false
        call apera:apera_inspect(sPipeline,"",nClassesFound,sClassID,nPartsDet)
      endIf
      //Check if there are parts to pick in the APERA's field of view
      //___CTC 12/9
      //  NORMAL  ///if nPartsDet!=0
      //  Testing AperaPartsFound loop count to total parts found_____CTC 12/9
      //--- Routing on the kick path. An earlier revision of mine FORCED the
      //  belt-index branch here on the theory that only that path stops the
      //  belt. That is no longer true and the force is removed: on the kick
      //  path tkBeltScan has already
      //  stopped the belt and AperaPartsFound has re-counted a settled scene,
      //  so a non-zero count means "carry on picking". Forcing the belt branch
      //  here (an earlier version of mine did) would switch the conveyor back
      //  ON right after the scan had just stopped it.
      if nCountPlatesFound!=0
        //Added Pounce position. keep robot moving while APERA Process capture data
        //movej(jTrajectoryStart,tSchunkEMH084,mCycle)
        //Check IF EndCycle button was pressed, then move to robot to Home position
        if bEndCycleRobot
          l_bEnding=true
        endIf
        if l_bEnding==true
          //  BELT: not touched - see the note at the socket-failure exit above.
          wait(isEmpty())
          call moveToHome()
        endIf
        //
        //No parts detected
      else
        //Early belt advance: consume the flag - the belt is handled from here on.
        //  l_bKickPath remembers we got here via the kick, after the global is
        //  cleared, so the blocks below can tell the two entries apart.
        l_bKickPath=bEarlyKickFired
        bEarlyKickFired=false
        //--- END CYCLE, second pulse source (Sep 2026, Cowork/Claude) ---------
        //  This was unconditional, and it sits BEFORE the bEndCycleRobot latch
        //  ~25 lines down. So on End Cycle the conveyor was switched ON here
        //  and only switched off again after the movej to jApera_Trigger_pos
        //  had completed - roughly a second of belt run AFTER the operator
        //  ended the cycle, and one more thing telling the PLC the cell wants
        //  the conveyor going (on T1 Output3 drives the PLC signal as well as
        //  belt power - see the I/O note in the BeltStop_Fix README).
        //  Same gate already used on the burst resume below. l_bEnding is in
        //  the test too because the scan watchdog above can latch it without
        //  bEndCycleRobot ever having been pressed.
        //--- BELT REMOVED HERE, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) ----------------------
        //  This used to switch the conveyor ON because the scene had just gone
        //  empty. That inference is exactly what this change removes: an empty
        //  scene is APERA's opinion, and on 11 Sep APERA reported a plate that
        //  was not on the belt on 180 of 180 frames. The belt now runs because
        //  the laser beam is clear and for no other reason, so there is
        //  nothing to command here. tkBeltSensor has already started it.
        //+++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++Part is already dropped. waiting for magnet is running belt without taking new pictures+++CTC 6/21
        //        movej(pDropPos,tSchunkEMH084,mCycle)
        //        call tMagnet("OFF")
        l_bCycleDone=true
        //--- Cycle Time: update the 10/25/50/100/1000-sample rolling windows -----
        //  l_bKickPath guards this pre-existing SECOND measurement. _nTimer was
        //  reset only a few lines above, so on the early-kick path this records
