        //  a near-zero "cycle" every last-part pick and drags Best/Last down.
        //  Off the kick path it behaves exactly as before.
        if _nTimer!=0 and l_bKickPath==false
          l_nElapsed=clock()-_nTimer
          call UpdateCycleWindow(_nCycleTime,_nCycleTimeIndex,l_nElapsed,"",true)
          call UpdateCycleWindow(_nCycleTime25,_nCycleTimeIndex25,l_nElapsed,"25",true)
          call UpdateCycleWindow(_nCycleTime50,_nCycleTimeIndex50,l_nElapsed,"50",true)
          call UpdateCycleWindow(_nCycleTime100,_nCycleTimeIndex100,l_nElapsed,"100",true)
          call UpdateCycleWindow(_nCycleTime1000,_nCycleTimeIndex1000,l_nElapsed,"1000",true)
        endIf
        //
        if l_bKickPath==false
          _nTimer=clock()
        endIf
        //
        movej(jApera_Trigger_pos,tAR70,mCycle)
        waitEndMove()
        //Check IF EndCycle button was pressed, then move to robot to Home position
        //  Latch it. moveToHome() calls init(), which clears bEndCycleRobot -
        //  so the old code cleared its own condition mid-loop. l_bEnding is a
        //  program local; init() cannot touch it.
        if bEndCycleRobot
          l_bEnding=true
        endIf
        //
        //Execute this until APERA detect parts
        //  The bScanDone term is a socket assertion, not an optimisation, and
        //  in practice it is always already satisfied here: reaching this line
        //  means either the fast path (which required an acknowledged socket)
        //  or the deferred block, which exits with an acknowledgement on both
        //  its normal and its watchdog path. It is written explicitly because
        //  the detect loop below calls apera_* and must not run against a
        //  live scan. Note what this line does NOT cover: the
        //  trigger / AperaPartsFound / query tail further down sits OUTSIDE
        //  this endIf and is protected only by l_bEnding. That is sufficient
        //  today - both entry paths into this branch have already proved
        //  ownership, and no re-claim is possible afterwards because
        //  AperaOwnSocket and Part_Accept both refuse while a kick is pending
        //  - but it is an argument, not an enforced invariant, so treat the
        //  tail as needing its own guard if you change the flow here.
        if l_bEnding==false and bScanForParts==false and bScanDone==true
          //--- Belt-runaway cap ---------------------------------------------------
          //  This loop used to run the conveyor with no limit, so a genuinely
          //  empty feeder ran the belt forever. It now runs in bursts of
          //  nDetectorTries; a failed burst STOPS the belt, raises/logs
          //  bFeederStarved, then retries. It never gives up, so the cell still
          //  recovers on its own when parts arrive.
          //  nDetectorTries = 0 disables the cap entirely (ships that way).
          //  The while and the resume below both test l_bEnding and bStart, so
          //  the cap can never override an End Cycle belt stop.
          //--- SEQUENCE FIX Sep 2026 -----------------------------------------------
          //  Force a fresh detect. nCountPlatesFound arriving here was set by
          //  AperaPartsFound() back during the Place+Return move - a camera read
          //  taken with the belt already running and the arm still moving. On the
          //  early-kick path we force our way into this branch regardless of that
          //  count, so a stale non-zero value made the "while count==0" wrapper
          //  skip the detect loop ENTIRELY: the belt ran for the whole place
          //  cycle, stopped, and went straight to the trajectory capture with no
          //  detect-based stop at all. That is the overflow path.
          //  The original code was a bare do..until, which always ran at least
          //  one detect; the runaway-cap wrapper is what allowed zero. Zeroing
          //  here restores "always detect at least once" for both paths (off the
          //  kick path this value is already 0, so it is a no-op there).
          nCountPlatesFound=0
          while nCountPlatesFound==0 and l_bEnding==false and bStart==true
            l_nTries=0
            //--- SENSOR-ONLY BELT, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) --------------------
            //  Was: run the conveyor and poll the camera until it sees plates,
            //  stopping the belt in bursts. The camera decided when to stop.
            //  Now: the beam decides. WaitBeltParts blocks until tkBeltSensor
            //  has a plate at the pick end AND has stopped and settled the
            //  belt; WaitFovClear then gets the arm out of shot. Only then is a
            //  picture taken, and it is ONE picture, not a poll - there is
            //  nothing to poll for, because the belt is already stopped on a
            //  physical detection.
            call WaitBeltParts(l_bBeltReady)
            call WaitFovClear(l_bFovClear)
            if l_bBeltReady==false or l_bFovClear==false
              //  Not ready: stood down, timed out, alarmed, or the arm could
              //  not clear the camera. Re-test the exits and go round again.
              //  The delay matters - on a latched beam alarm WaitBeltParts
              //  returns immediately and this would otherwise spin flat out.
              call BeltAlarmHold(l_nAlarmClk)
            endIf
            do
              if bEndCycleRobot
                l_bEnding=true
              endIf
              //  Zero before the read - apera_inspect leaves its output array
              //  UNTOUCHED on a successful read of an empty scene (its clearing
              //  loop is commented out and its write loop does not run when zero
              //  classes are found), so the sum below would re-add the previous
              //  reading. bAperaError is false on that path. See tkBeltScan.pgx.
              nPartsDet[0]=0
              nPartsDet[1]=0
              nPartsDet[2]=0
              nPartsDet[3]=0
              nPartsDet[4]=0
              call apera:apera_trigger("14in_plate det")
              call apera:apera_wait_for_capture("14in_plate det","")
              call apera:apera_inspect("14in_plate det","",nClassesFound,sClassID,nPartsDet)
              //--- Socket-error guard, added Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) -------------
              //  apera_inspect RETURNS EARLY on a socket error WITHOUT writing
              //  nPartsDet (apera/apera_inspect.pgx: "if bAperaError==true /
              //  return" sits above the write to x_nObjectCount). So this sum
              //  would otherwise re-add the PREVIOUS reading: a stale non-zero
              //  ends the loop and stops the belt on an empty scene, a stale
              //  zero spins. Treat an error as "no reading".
              //  apera:bAperaError is the library's public flag. Note the bare
              //  bAperaError tested at the top of this program resolves to this
              //  project's OWN private copy (declared in the .dtx), which
              //  nothing ever writes - that check is dead. Pre-existing; not
              //  touched here, but it is why this one is qualified.
              //  THIS IS A BEHAVIOUR CHANGE to a long-standing loop. It only
              //  ever suppresses a reading that the library itself flagged as
              //  failed, but it is a change, so it is called out in the README.
              if apera:bAperaError==true
                nCountPlatesFound=0
                delay(0.2)
              else
                nCountPlatesFound=nPartsDet[0]+nPartsDet[1]+nPartsDet[2]
                sFounds=" "+toString("",nCountPlatesFound)+" TiePlate(s)"
              endIf
              l_nTries=l_nTries+1
              delay(0)
              //NORMAL//  until(nPartsDet!=0)
              //  Exits on End Cycle now. Previously only "parts found" ended this
              //  loop - with the belt forced off no part could ever arrive, so
              //  End Cycle on an empty feeder could spin here indefinitely,
              //  re-homing every pass.
            until (nCountPlatesFound!=0 or l_bEnding==true or bStart==false or (nDetectorTries>0 and l_nTries>=nDetectorTries) or (bBeltPartAtEnd==true and apera:bAperaError==false))
            //--- NOTHING PICKABLE ON A BROKEN BEAM --------------------------
            //  The belt is stopped because something is physically at the pick
            //  end, and the camera cannot pick it. That is the one deadlock
            //  sensor-only control creates, so it is counted and handed to
            //  tkBeltSensor, which owns the nudge and the alarm. This task
            //  must NOT run the belt to clear it - one writer.
            if nCountPlatesFound==0 and l_bEnding==false and bStart==true and bBeltPartAtEnd==true and apera:bAperaError==false
              nNoPickOnBeam=nNoPickOnBeam+1
              //  v2.1.0 (Oct 2026): withhold the next capture until tkBeltSensor
              //  has jogged the belt for this miss. Only ever CLEARS the flag;
              //  tkBeltSensor re-asserts it once the jog is done and settled.
              bBeltReadyForCapture=false
              bFeederStarved=true
            endIf
          endWhile
        endIf
        //  BELT: not touched. tkBeltSensor stopped it when the plate broke the
        //  beam, and will start it again when the beam clears.
        if l_bEnding==true
          //  Home once, not once per loop pass. moveToHome() -> init() also
          //  drops bStart, so the outer while() ends the task cleanly.
          wait(isEmpty())
          call moveToHome()
        else
          //delay Camera untill parts are setteled once belt stops__________________CTC 6/21
          //  LEFT AT THE ORIGINAL 0.5. I had swapped this for nPartsSettle
          //  and put it back: this branch runs on every ordinary empty-scene
          //  cycle whether or not the early kick is enabled, and nPartsSettle
          //  ships at 1.0 - so that swap silently added half a second per pass
          //  to normal production. nPartsSettle now applies only to the new
          //  kick path (tkBeltScan), which is what it was asked for.
          delay(0.5)
          //--- Robot reported inside a camera FOV cube at capture time ----------
          //  DIAGNOSTIC ONLY - does not wait. The movej to jApera_Trigger_pos
          //  above is followed by waitEndMove(), so the arm has already
          //  stopped at the taught out-of-shot pose by the time we get here;
          //  a wait would add nothing but could add nFovClearTimeout_s of
          //  latency to every empty-scene cycle if that pose turns out to
          //  overlap a cube. Log it instead, and the log tells you the
          //  geometry needs a look.
          //  Counted per cube, not just logged, so the geometry problem can be
          //  pinned to ONE cube. The 2026-09-02 log proved the arm IS reported
          //  inside a cube at this capture on some picks - but not which one, and
          //  the controller's log de-duplication hid how often. Counters do not
          //  de-duplicate.
          if _bStartFoV==true
            nFovHitStart=nFovHitStart+1
          endIf
          if _bEndFoV==true
            nFovHitEnd=nFovHitEnd+1
          endIf
          //  Named in words rather than by concatenating the flags: toString() in
          //  VAL3 formats a num, NOT a bool, so toString("",_bStartFoV) does not
          //  compile ("invalid type") - which is exactly how the first version of
          //  this line failed on the controller.
          if _bStartFoV==true and _bEndFoV==true
            logMsg("RAIV/T1: capture with arm inside BOTH FoV cubes, nCount="+toString("",nCountPlatesFound),1)
          elseIf _bStartFoV==true
            logMsg("RAIV/T1: capture with arm INSIDE the APERA view - this capture images the gripper, nCount="+toString("",nCountPlatesFound),1)
          elseIf _bEndFoV==true
            logMsg("RAIV/T1: capture with arm PAST the APERA view (end zone), nCount="+toString("",nCountPlatesFound),1)
          endIf
          //  Sensor-only belt: see the matching note in the other branch tail.
          call WaitBeltParts(l_bBeltReady)
          call WaitFovClear(l_bFovClear)
          //  v2.2.0: remember the belt state this picture is taken in.
          nTrajBeltStarts=nBeltMasterStarts
          bTrajBeltOk=bBeltReadyForCapture
          call apera:apera_trigger_trajectory(sPipeline,jTrajectoryStart,jTrajectoryEnd)
          _nTrigClk=clock()
          call AperaPartsFound()
          // +++++++Belt was double indexing without instruction to wait for trajectory+++++++++++++ CTC 6/27
          call apera:apera_wait_for_trajectory(sPipeline,sPipelineGroup,bTrajectoryFound,pPose,pPoseTransform,nPickPointID)
        endIf
      endIf
      //
    else
      // +++++++++++++++++++++++++++No Pickable Parts Found++++++++++++++CTC 6/25
      //*******************Change pipeline, and count # parts *****************************
      //--- BRANCH B TIMING, Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude) --------------------------
      //  ***  WHAT RUN 19 ESTABLISHED, AND WHAT IT CORRECTED  ***
      //  Steel + belt, 308 picks: 47 picks carried 3.5-9 s that no segment
      //  accounted for. ALL 47 - 100%, not most - were the pick immediately
      //  after a pass through THIS branch. nBranchBPasses went 2 -> 67: 65
      //  passes, one every 4.7 picks, median 3.86 s, 209 s total, which is
      //  +0.68 s amortised onto EVERY pick. That is bigger than anything left
      //  in the motion path.
      //  I first reported this as the empty-scene re-scan in the branch above.
      //  That was WRONG and the log says so plainly: nCountPlatesFound never
      //  logged 0 in the entire run (1..9, never 0), so that branch never ran.
      //  This is the bTrajectoryFound==false path - APERA SEES plates and
      //  plans no pickable trajectory for any of them. Different cause,
      //  different fix. The 2026-09-02 note already in this branch said "~24 s
      //  on roughly every fourth pick"; run 19 is the same branch, measured.
      //  WHY THIS IS TIMING ONLY. The branch is four things in a row and the
      //  log cannot currently tell them apart:
      //    travel  movej(jApera_Trigger_pos) + waitEndMove   - real motion
      //    detect  belt ON + the apera_trigger/capture/inspect loop
      //    settle  the delay below, now nBranchBSettle_s
      //    traj    trigger + AperaPartsFound + wait_for_trajectory, ARM PARKED
      //  Only the last one is obviously wasteful - the main path hides that
      //  same wait behind movej(jTrajectoryStart,mReach) and this one does
      //  not. But "obviously" is how the Capture @ FoV result went wrong on
      //  steel, so it gets measured before anything moves.
      //  Nothing here adds, removes or reorders a move, an output or a wait.
      l_nBrT0=clock()
      l_nBrDet=0
      nBranchBPlates=nCountPlatesFound
      l_bCycleDone=false
      movej(jApera_Trigger_pos,tAR70,mCycle)
      waitEndMove()
      l_nBrT1=clock()
      //  END CYCLE. This whole branch previously had no bEndCycleRobot check
      //  of any kind, so the belt was switched on below and ran until parts
      //  were found - End Cycle or not. That is the other half of why the
      //  conveyor behaved differently depending on which branch was active.
      //  Note this latches on bEndCycleRobot ALONE, not "bEndCycleRobot and
      //  l_bCycleDone" as the other branch does: l_bCycleDone is set false on
      //  entry to this branch, so that idiom could never be true here.
      if bEndCycleRobot
        l_bEnding=true
      endIf
      //  Count passes through this branch. The 2026-09-02 log showed the cell
      //  looping here for ~24 s on roughly every fourth pick, visible only as
      //  the controller collapsing a repeated message into "the previous
      //  message appeared N times". A counter makes it measurable.
      nBranchBPasses=nBranchBPasses+1
      //--- Own the APERA socket for this branch (Sep 2026) --------------------
      //  Everything below in this branch calls apera_* - the detect loop and
      //  the trigger / AperaPartsFound / query tail after it - and none of it
      //  may run against a live scan. In today's flow the scan is always
      //  already resolved before we get here, but this branch is the one a
      //  future edit will break, and it had no socket term of any kind.
      //  If the socket cannot be obtained, latch the end: AperaOwnSocket has
      //  already dropped the belt, and the l_bEnding path below homes the
      //  robot without making a single apera call.
      call AperaOwnSocket(l_bSocketFree)
      if l_bSocketFree==false
        l_bEnding=true
      endIf
      //  bStart added Sep 2026 as a DEFENSIVE term, not as a fix for an
      //  observed mechanism - I want to be honest about that, because I first
      //  wrote it up as "Stop drops bStart without setting bEndCycleRobot".
      //  It does not: bStart=false is written in exactly one place in the
      //  whole project, init.pgx, reached only from moveToHome() (End Cycle)
      //  and clickAbort() (Abort), both of which do involve bEndCycleRobot or
      //  bAbort. Everything in this block commands the conveyor, so testing
      //  bStart as well costs nothing and closes the case where that ever
      //  changes.
      if l_bEnding==false and bStart==true
        //--- "NO TRAJECTORY FOUND" NO LONGER DRIVES THE BELT -------------------
        //  Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude), requested by Coty. This branch used to
        //  pulse the conveyor for a second after two consecutive failures and
        //  then run it until the camera saw something. Both are gone. APERA
        //  failing to plan a pick is a statement about the PLAN, not about
        //  whether there is anything on the belt, and treating it as a belt
        //  command is how the cell came to advance the conveyor on scenes that
        //  still had plates in them.
        //  l_nNOTrajectoryFound is still counted, and it still routes the
        //  cycle - it just does not touch the conveyor any more.
        //  What DOES happen here: if the beam is broken, the thing at the pick
        //  end could not be planned, so that failure is reported to the belt
        //  master, which owns the nudge and the alarm.
        if bBeltPartAtEnd==true
          nNoPickOnBeam=nNoPickOnBeam+1
          //  v2.1.0: hold the next capture until the jog for this miss is done.
          bBeltReadyForCapture=false
        endIf
        nCountPlatesFound=0
        while nCountPlatesFound==0 and l_bEnding==false and bStart==true
          l_nTries=0
          //  Sensor-only: wait for a plate at the beam and a stopped, settled
          //  belt, then get out of the camera's view. See the matching note in
          //  the branch above.
          call WaitBeltParts(l_bBeltReady)
          call WaitFovClear(l_bFovClear)
          if l_bBeltReady==false or l_bFovClear==false
            call BeltAlarmHold(l_nAlarmClk)
          endIf
          do
            if bEndCycleRobot
              l_bEnding=true
            endIf
            //  Zero before the read - apera_inspect leaves its output array
            //  UNTOUCHED on a successful read of an empty scene (its clearing
            //  loop is commented out and its write loop does not run when zero
            //  classes are found), so the sum below would re-add the previous
            //  reading. bAperaError is false on that path. See tkBeltScan.pgx.
            nPartsDet[0]=0
            nPartsDet[1]=0
            nPartsDet[2]=0
            nPartsDet[3]=0
            nPartsDet[4]=0
            call apera:apera_trigger("14in_plate det")
            call apera:apera_wait_for_capture("14in_plate det","")
            call apera:apera_inspect("14in_plate det","",nClassesFound,sClassID,nPartsDet)
            //--- Socket-error guard - see the matching note in the branch above.
            if apera:bAperaError==true
              nCountPlatesFound=0
              delay(0.2)
            else
              nCountPlatesFound=nPartsDet[0]+nPartsDet[1]+nPartsDet[2]
              sFounds=" "+toString("",nCountPlatesFound)+" TiePlate(s)"
            endIf
            l_nTries=l_nTries+1
            //  Branch B only: total detector round trips across ALL passes of
            //  the outer while, where l_nTries restarts at 0 on each pass. The
            //  detector loop is a full camera capture per iteration, so "how
            //  many" is half the answer to "why did detect take that long".
            l_nBrDet=l_nBrDet+1
            delay(0)
            //NORMAL//  until(nPartsDet!=0)
          until (nCountPlatesFound!=0 or l_bEnding==true or bStart==false or (nDetectorTries>0 and l_nTries>=nDetectorTries) or (bBeltPartAtEnd==true and apera:bAperaError==false))
          //  Nothing pickable on a broken beam - see the matching note above.
          if nCountPlatesFound==0 and l_bEnding==false and bStart==true and bBeltPartAtEnd==true and apera:bAperaError==false
            nNoPickOnBeam=nNoPickOnBeam+1
            //  v2.1.0 - see the matching note in the branch above.
            bBeltReadyForCapture=false
            bFeederStarved=true
          endIf
        endWhile
      endIf
      //  BELT: not touched - tkBeltSensor owns it.
      l_nBrT2=clock()
      if l_bEnding==true
        wait(isEmpty())
        call moveToHome()
      else
        //wait for Conveyor to stop moving before taking another image_____________________ CTC 6/21
        //  LEFT AT THE ORIGINAL 0.5 - see the matching note in the branch
        //  above. nPartsSettle applies to the new kick path only.
        //  MADE TUNABLE Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude): nBranchBSettle_s SHIPS AT
        //  0.5, which is exactly the number that was hard-coded here, so this
        //  is behaviour-neutral until somebody changes it from SP2. It is
        //  separate from nPartsSettle on purpose - that one belongs to the
        //  kick path and this branch is not the kick path.
        //  This settle exists so the belt has stopped before the capture. It
        //  is a real requirement, not slack: shortening it trades image
        //  quality for time, and a blurred capture here costs a whole extra
        //  Branch B pass. Sweep it downward only with the plates_found and
        //  APERA-error lines in view, the same discipline as the FoV bound.
        //  Clamped into a LOCAL, not back onto the tunable: code in this
        //  project does not write a value the operator owns. A silly entry is
        //  ignored for this pass and still reads back as what was typed, so
        //  the pendant never disagrees with the data editor.
        l_nBrSettle=nBranchBSettle_s
        if l_nBrSettle<0
          l_nBrSettle=0
        endIf
        if l_nBrSettle>5
          l_nBrSettle=5
        endIf
        delay(l_nBrSettle)
        l_nBrT3=clock()
        //--- Robot reported inside a camera FOV cube at capture time ----------
        //  DIAGNOSTIC ONLY - see the matching note in the branch above.
        //  Counted per cube, not just logged, so the geometry problem can be
        //  pinned to ONE cube. The 2026-09-02 log proved the arm IS reported
        //  inside a cube at this capture on some picks - but not which one, and
        //  the controller's log de-duplication hid how often. Counters do not
        //  de-duplicate.
        if _bStartFoV==true
          nFovHitStart=nFovHitStart+1
        endIf
        if _bEndFoV==true
          nFovHitEnd=nFovHitEnd+1
        endIf
        //  Named in words rather than by concatenating the flags: toString() in
        //  VAL3 formats a num, NOT a bool, so toString("",_bStartFoV) does not
        //  compile ("invalid type") - which is exactly how the first version of
        //  this line failed on the controller.
        if _bStartFoV==true and _bEndFoV==true
          logMsg("RAIV/T1: capture with arm inside BOTH FoV cubes, nCount="+toString("",nCountPlatesFound),1)
        elseIf _bStartFoV==true
          logMsg("RAIV/T1: capture with arm INSIDE the APERA view - this capture images the gripper, nCount="+toString("",nCountPlatesFound),1)
        elseIf _bEndFoV==true
          logMsg("RAIV/T1: capture with arm PAST the APERA view (end zone), nCount="+toString("",nCountPlatesFound),1)
        endIf
        //  Sensor-only belt: prove BOTH preconditions at the shutter rather
        //  than arguing them. The detect loop above did wait for a stopped,
        //  settled belt, and the beam cannot have cleared since - nothing has
        //  been picked - but that is a chain of reasoning, and the next edit
        //  to this branch will not know it. WaitBeltParts returns immediately
        //  when the belt is already stopped and ready, so re-asking costs one
        //  flag read and turns the argument into a check.
        call WaitBeltParts(l_bBeltReady)
        call WaitFovClear(l_bFovClear)
        //  v2.2.0: remember the belt state this picture is taken in.
        nTrajBeltStarts=nBeltMasterStarts
        bTrajBeltOk=bBeltReadyForCapture
        call apera:apera_trigger_trajectory(sPipeline,jTrajectoryStart,jTrajectoryEnd)
        _nTrigClk=clock()
        call AperaPartsFound()
        //_____________________+++++++++++++++++++++++++Wait till triagectory found +++++++++CTC 6/25
        //call apera:apera_trigger_trajectory(sPipeline,jTrajectoryStart,jTrajectoryEnd)
        call apera:apera_wait_for_trajectory(sPipeline,sPipelineGroup,bTrajectoryFound,pPose,pPoseTransform,nPickPointID)
        //--- Publish this Branch B pass. MEASUREMENT ONLY ----------------------
        //  Published here rather than logged here: LogCycleCSV is not called on
        //  this branch (no pick completed, so there is no cycle to log), and
        //  calling it would invent a cycle row. These globals therefore hold
        //  the LAST Branch B pass and are logged as line K on the NEXT pick -
        //  which is the same pick that carries the unaccounted seconds, so the
        //  two line up in the parser without any extra work.
        //  nBranchBTotal_s is the sum of the four parts by construction
        //  (T3-T0 plus the traj window), not an independent stamp, so the
        //  parts always add up to it and a disagreement means a code error.
        nBranchBTravel_s=l_nBrT1-l_nBrT0
        nBranchBDetect_s=l_nBrT2-l_nBrT1
        nBranchBSettleMeas_s=l_nBrT3-l_nBrT2
        nBranchBTraj_s=clock()-l_nBrT3
        nBranchBTotal_s=clock()-l_nBrT0
        nBranchBTries=l_nBrDet
        nBranchBTotalAll_s=nBranchBTotalAll_s+nBranchBTotal_s
      endIf
      //++++++++++++++++++++++++++++^^^^^^^^^^^^CTC 6/25
      l_nNOTrajectoryFound=l_nNOTrajectoryFound+1
    endIf

    //Note: cycle-time and per-segment stats are now updated inline (via
    //UpdateCycleWindow) at the exact points above where each sample is
    //known, rather than being deferred and detected here. See History.
    //
    delay(0)
  endWhile
  //  //At cycle end the robot goes back to the homing position
  //  movej(jHome,tSchunkEMH084,mSlow)
  //  waitEndMove()

  //  if !bStart
  //    bEndCycleRobot=true
  //  else
  //    bStart=false
  //  endIf

  //nCycleRemain=nCycle
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: tkIOMonitor.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="tkIOMonitor" access="private" >
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_bPwrOk" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bPwrPending" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bPwrWas" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bStartPB" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bStartWas" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bStalled" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bTcpInit" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nDist" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nDt" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nPwrTryClk" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nStallDur" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nStallT0" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nStallX0" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_nTcpT" type="num" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_pTcpNow" type="point" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_pTcpPrev" type="point" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //  v2.2.0 (Oct 2026, Cowork/Claude): ARM POWER IS REQUESTED ON AN EDGE ONLY.
  //  Until v2.0.0 enablePower() was called on every pass (delay(0)) while the
  //  NC servo switch read 1. v2.1.0 throttled that to once per 2 s - and the
  //  20 Oct backup proved it was not enough: ONE request made while a safety
  //  restart is pending stays pending, and the CONTROLLER logs the refusal
  //  every 4 ms ("Cannot apply arm power" / "a safety restart is needed")
  //  until the restart is done. esStatus() reads 0 in that state, so it
  //  cannot be used to detect it.
  //  Now: enablePower() is called only on the OFF->ON edge of the servo
  //  switch (with no Abort) or on a press of the Start pushbutton, and only if
  //  the arm is not powered. If power is not on within nPowerRetry_s (2 s)
  //  the request is WITHDRAWN with disablePower() and one message tells the
  //  operator to reset the safety and cycle the switch. Opening the app never
  //  requests power by itself - the edge memories are seeded from the
  //  current inputs.
  l_bPwrOk=(dioGet(dDIPowerEnable)==1 and dioGet(dDIAbort)==0 and dioGet(dDIAbortRemote)==0)
  l_bPwrWas=l_bPwrOk
  l_bStartWas=(dioGet(dDIStartCycle[0])==1 or dioGet(dDIStartRemote)==1)
  l_bPwrPending=false
  l_nPwrTryClk=clock()
  while true
    //--------------------
    //  Inputs
    //--------------------   
    //  Tie Plate Selection Start......
    if dioGet(dDI14in_tieplate)==1
      sPipeline="Pick_IdsBelt_new"
      sTiePlateUser="14in"
    elseIf dioGet(dDI18in_tieplate)==1
      sPipeline="Pick_IdsBelt_new"
      sTiePlateUser="18in"
    else
      sPipeline="Pick_IdsBelt_new"
      sTiePlateUser="Pandral"
    endIf
    //_______Old Logic Below____ CTC 12/23
    //    if dioGet(dDI14in_tieplate)==0
    //      bINPUTS["Input1"]=false
    //    else
    //      bINPUTS["Input1"]=true
    //      sPipeline="Pick_IdsBelt"
    //      sTiePlateUser="14in"
    //    endIf
    //    if dioGet(dDI18in_tieplate)==0
    //      bINPUTS["Input2"]=false
    //    else
    //      bINPUTS["Input2"]=true
    //      sPipeline="Pick_IdsBelt"
    //      sTiePlateUser="18in"
    //    endIf
    //_____________^^^__Old Logic__CTC 12/23
    //  Tie Plate Selection END.....
    //
    //____________________________________Created abort with wireless remote CTC 8/16
    if dioGet(dDIAbort)==0 and dioGet(dDIAbortRemote)==0
      bINPUTS["Input3"]=false
    else
      bINPUTS["Input3"]=true
      bAbort=true
    endIf
    //+++++++++++Input Ch. 4 ++++++++++++++++++++Added physical pushbutton to end cycle, stop belt, stow robot++++CTC 7/19
    //Wireless remote option added CTC 8/16
    if dioGet(dDIEndCycle)==0 and dioGet(dDIEndCycleRemote)==0
      bINPUTS["Input4"]=false
    else
      bINPUTS["Input4"]=true
      bEndCycleRobot=true
    endIf
    //^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
    //______________________________________________________allow Abort button to disable power. wireless remote option added.  CTC 8/16
    l_bPwrOk=(dioGet(dDIPowerEnable)==1 and dioGet(dDIAbort)==0 and dioGet(dDIAbortRemote)==0)
    l_bStartPB=(dioGet(dDIStartCycle[0])==1 or dioGet(dDIStartRemote)==1)
    if l_bPwrOk==true
      bINPUTS["Input5"]=true
      if isPowered()==false and l_bPwrPending==false
        if l_bPwrWas==false or (l_bStartPB==true and l_bStartWas==false)
          enablePower()
          l_bPwrPending=true
          l_nPwrTryClk=clock()
        endIf
      endIf
      if l_bPwrPending==true
        if isPowered()==true
          l_bPwrPending=false
        elseIf (clock()-l_nPwrTryClk)>=nPowerRetry_s
          l_bPwrPending=false
          disablePower()
          logMsg("RAIV/T1: arm power refused - reset the safety circuit, then turn the servo switch OFF and ON (or press Start).",2)
        endIf
      endIf
    else
      bINPUTS["Input5"]=false
      l_bPwrPending=false
      if isPowered()==true
        disablePower()
      endIf
      dioSet(dDOAirStream,0)
    endIf
    l_bPwrWas=l_bPwrOk
    l_bStartWas=l_bStartPB
    if dioGet(dDI100Speed)==0
      bINPUTS["Input6"]=false
    else
      bINPUTS["Input6"]=true
      _nSpeed=100
      setMonitorSpeed(_nSpeed)
    endIf
    //+++++++++Input Ch. 7 +++++++++++++++Add Physical pushbutton to Start Cycle+++++++++++CTC 7/19
    if dioGet(dDIStartCycle[0])==0 and dioGet(dDIStartRemote)==0
      bINPUTS["Input7"]=false
    else
      bINPUTS["Input7"]=true
      bStart=true
    endIf
    if dioGet(dDI25Speed)==0
      bINPUTS["Input8"]=false
    else
      bINPUTS["Input8"]=true
      _nSpeed=25
      setMonitorSpeed(_nSpeed)
    endIf
    //^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
    //=====================================================================
    //  WHAT SPEED IS THE ROBOT ACTUALLY RUNNING AT?  Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude)
    //  READ ONLY. Neither setMonitorSpeed() call above is touched, their
    //  order is unchanged, and nothing below writes a speed.
    //
    //  WHY THIS EXISTS. From the VAL3 reference:
    //      "setMonitorSpeed() is always able to REDUCE the monitor speed. To
    //       INCREASE it, setMonitorSpeed() is effective only if the robot is
    //       in remote working mode and if the operator does not have access
    //       to the monitor speed (when the current user profile does not
    //       allow use of the speed buttons, or the MCP has been disconnected)."
    //  So the two calls above are NOT symmetric:
    //      dDI25Speed  -> setMonitorSpeed(25)  is a REDUCTION: always works.
    //      dDI100Speed -> setMonitorSpeed(100) is an INCREASE: conditional.
    //  With the PENDANT CONNECTED the operator has access to the monitor
    //  speed, so the 100% selector CANNOT raise it - the press is accepted
    //  and silently does nothing. With the pendant disconnected, which is how
    //  this cell runs in production, it works. The 100% button therefore
    //  works in production and fails while anyone is testing at the pendant,
    //  which is the opposite of what you would guess.
    //  That is not theoretical here: every run from 2026-09-04 to 09-08 was a
    //  pendant-connected test run and every one of them sat at reduced speed
    //  (descend 0.50-0.89 s against 0.37 s at full), while the only sustained
    //  full-speed production data we have is 09-03. To RAISE the speed while
    //  the pendant is connected, use the PENDANT'S OWN speed control - that
    //  is the access which is blocking the instruction.
    //
    //  AND _nSpeed IS NOT THE ANSWER. It is written immediately before each
    //  call, unconditionally, so it records what was REQUESTED, not what took
    //  effect - and it ships at 100 in the .dtx, so it reads 100 from a cold
    //  boot at any actual speed. getMonitorSpeed() is the only thing that
    //  knows, and before this it was called NOWHERE in the project: the cell
    //  had no idea what speed it was running at and neither did the log.
    //
    //  nMonitorSpeedActual is published every pass (a getter, no side effect),
    //  logged per pick on CSV line H beside _nSpeed, and shown on the Options
    //  page. The pair is the diagnosis: requested 100, actual 25 means the
    //  instruction was refused.
    //=====================================================================
    nMonitorSpeedActual=getMonitorSpeed()
    //  The refusal alarm, latched so a delay(0) loop cannot spam the log.
    //  bSpeedNotApplied is true for exactly as long as an unresolved mismatch
    //  stands, which is what makes it both the log latch and the lamp on the
    //  Options page. Written as nested ifs, never as one "and" expression:
    //  VAL3's reference does not promise short-circuit evaluation.
    //  The message carries no percent sign and is ~190 bytes on purpose:
    //  logMsg is capped at 255 bytes and truncates SILENTLY, and a percent
    //  in a logged string is not worth the risk of format handling when it
    //  cannot be tested from here. No other message in this project uses one.
    if dioGet(dDI100Speed)==1
      if nMonitorSpeedActual<nSpeedWarnBelow
        if bSpeedNotApplied==false
          bSpeedNotApplied=true
          nSpeedRejects=nSpeedRejects+1
          logMsg("RAIV/T1: the 100 speed selector is ON but getMonitorSpeed() reads "+toString("",nMonitorSpeedActual)+" - setMonitorSpeed() can only RAISE speed with the pendant DISCONNECTED. Raise it at the pendant, or unplug the pendant.",2)
        endIf
      else
        bSpeedNotApplied=false
      endIf
    else
      bSpeedNotApplied=false
    endIf
    //--------------------
    //  Outputs
    //--------------------   
    if bOUTPUTS["Output1"] or isPowered()
      dioSet(ServoLamp,1)
    else
      dioSet(ServoLamp,0)
    endIf
    if bOUTPUTS["Output2"]
      dioSet(dDOOutput2,1)
    else
      dioSet(dDOOutput2,0)
    endIf
    //+++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++Added ispowered() to kill conveyor CTC 6/24
    //+++++++++Created Array for Conveyor on, [0] powering belt; [1] signaling to PLC CTC 8/16
    if bOUTPUTS["Output3"] and isPowered() and bAbort==false
      dioSet(dDOConveyorON[0],1)
      dioSet(dDOConveyorON[1],1)
    else
      dioSet(dDOConveyorON[0],0)
      dioSet(dDOConveyorON[1],0)
    endIf
    if bOUTPUTS["Output4"]
      dioSet(dDOWorkingLight,1)
    else
      dioSet(dDOWorkingLight,0)
    endIf
    if !isSettled()
      dioSet(dDOWorkingLight,1)
    else
      dioSet(dDOWorkingLight,0)
    endIf

    if bDryCycle==false
      dDOValve1Enable=true
    else
      dDOValve1Enable=false
    endIf

    //    if tTool.gripper==true
    //      dioSet(dDOTool,1)
    //    else
    //      dioSet(dDOTool,0)
    //    endIf
    //_______________________________ CTC 1/2
    if dioGet(dDOemh_gripperON)==1
      dioSet(dDOTool,1)
    endIf
    if dioGet(dDOemh_gripperOFF)==1
      dioSet(dDOTool,0)
    endIf

    //    if bOUTPUTS["Output5"]
    //      dioSet(dDOOutput5,1)
    //    else
    //      dioSet(dDOOutput5,0)
    //    endIf
    //
    //    if bOUTPUTS["Output6"]
    //      dioSet(dDOOutput6,1)
    //    else
    //      dioSet(dDOOutput6,0)
    //    endIf
    //    if bOUTPUTS["Output7"]
    //      dioSet(dDOOutput7,1)
    //    else
    //      dioSet(dDOOutput7,0)
    //    endIf
    //    if bOUTPUTS["Output8"]
    //      dioSet(dDOOutput8,1)
    //    else
    //      dioSet(dDOOutput8,0)
    //    endIf
    //
    //----------------------------Restart Safety Signal CTC 2/7
    if esStatus()==0
      dioSet(dDoSafetyStop_None,1)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_Waiting,0)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_SS1,0)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_SS2,0)
    elseIf esStatus()==1
      dioSet(dDoSafetyStop_None,0)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_Waiting,1)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_SS1,0)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_SS2,0)
    elseIf esStatus()==2
      dioSet(dDoSafetyStop_None,0)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_Waiting,0)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_SS1,1)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_SS2,0)
    elseIf esStatus()==3
      dioSet(dDoSafetyStop_None,0)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_Waiting,0)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_SS1,0)
      dioSet(dDoSafteyStop_SS2,1)
    endIf
    //=====================================================================
    //  TCP STALL SAMPLER - measurement only.  Sep 2026 (Cowork/Claude)
    //=====================================================================
    //  WHAT IT IS FOR. tkCycle stops the arm DEAD twice on the way out to a
    //  plate - waitEndMove() at L282 after APPROACH, and waitEndMove() plus
    //  wait(isSettled()) at L292 after LINEAR_APPROACH - while the way back
    //  in has no stop at all: LINEAR_RETREAT, RETREAT and Part_Accept's
    //  drop-approach movej queue back to back and blend.
    //
    //  The first of those stops exists only so that dioSet(dDOAirStream,1)
    //  happens between the two moves. Staubli's own MoveID material shows
    //  the alternative - assert the output at a PERCENTAGE of the move with
    //  getMoveId() and let the two blend - but that is the motion path, and
    //  I am not touching it on a guess. First: how many seconds a pick is
    //  actually spent standing still, and where.
    //
    //  HOW. Sample the TCP every pass, differentiate, and time every
    //  interval below nStallSpeed_mms. distance() and here() are reads with
    //  no side effect; nothing here writes an output or queues motion.
    //
    //  WHY IT IS AT THE BOTTOM OF THE LOOP AND GATED OFF. This task also
    //  services dDIAbort at the top of every pass, so anything that slows
    //  the loop slows the abort response. bStallSample ships FALSE and this
    //  reduces to one bool test; turn it on only with somebody at the cell.
    //
    //  AND THE SELF-CHECK. A stall shorter than one loop period is invisible
    //  to this, so the loop period is published too. If nMonLoopMax_ms is
    //  large the counts are floor values, not measurements - read it first.
    if bStallSample==true
      l_pTcpNow=here(tAR70,world)
      l_nDt=clock()-l_nTcpT
      if l_bTcpInit==true
        if l_nDt>0.0005
          nMonLoop_ms=l_nDt*1000
          if nMonLoop_ms>nMonLoopMax_ms
            nMonLoopMax_ms=nMonLoop_ms
          endIf
          l_nDist=distance(l_pTcpPrev,l_pTcpNow)
          nTcpSpeed_mms=l_nDist/l_nDt
          if nTcpSpeed_mms<nStallSpeed_mms
            if l_bStalled==false
              l_bStalled=true
              l_nStallT0=clock()
              l_nStallX0=l_pTcpNow.trsf.x
            endIf
          else
            if l_bStalled==true
              l_bStalled=false
              l_nStallDur=clock()-l_nStallT0
              //  nStallMin_s discards single-sample noise at the threshold.
              if l_nStallDur>=nStallMin_s
                nStallCount=nStallCount+1
                nStallTotal_s=nStallTotal_s+l_nStallDur
                nStallLast_s=l_nStallDur
                nStallLastX=l_nStallX0
                if l_nStallDur>nStallMax_s
                  nStallMax_s=l_nStallDur
                endIf
              endIf
            endIf
          endIf
        endIf
      endIf
      l_pTcpPrev=l_pTcpNow
      l_nTcpT=clock()
      l_bTcpInit=true
    else
      //  Switched off mid-stall: forget the partial episode rather than
      //  closing it later against a stale t0 and logging a huge duration.
      l_bTcpInit=false
      l_bStalled=false
    endIf
    //=====================================================================
    delay(0)
  endWhile
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: tkRobotAtHome.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="tkRobotAtHome" access="private" >
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  Monitor jHome position
  // bRobotAtHome comes TRUE when robot is +/- 5 degrees from this position otherwise bRobotAtHome comes FALSE
  //History
  //  Creation : June 2024 A. Cisneros (Stäubli)
  //Header end
  while true
    // Checks that all the axis are less than 5 degrees from the reference
    if (!(abs(herej()-jHome)<jMaxDistance))
      bRobotAtHome=false
    else
      bRobotAtHome=true
    endIf
    //
    if (!(abs(herej()-jStow)<jMaxDistance))
      bRobotAtStow=false
    else
      bRobotAtStow=true
    endIf
  endWhile
end]]></Code>

===== FILE: HMI/uCycleStats.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="uCycleStats" access="private" >
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_bPage" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />

    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  This is the "Cycle Timing" user interface (SP2 page 4).
  //      Displays:
  //              * Overall cycle-time rolling windows: Last 10 / 25 / 50 /
  //                100 / 1000 - Average, Best, Worse in Seconds and PPM,
  //                plus how full each window currently is (e.g. "12/25").
  //              * Per-segment timing: Approach, Linear Approach, Gripper
  //                Actuate, Retreat, Place+Return, Vision Wait - Last and
  //                Average seconds over the most recent 25 cycles.
  //      All of the numeric fields on this page are plain read-only
  //      bindings straight to _sCycleTime[] (see UserPageCycleStats
  //      .bindings.json) - same pattern as the existing cycle-time fields
  //      on the Production page - so no userPageSet() calls are needed
  //      here to keep them current.
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : Aug 2026 (Cowork/Claude)
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  userPage("UserPageCycleStats")
  l_bPage=false
  do
    //--------------------
    //  User Interface
    //--------------------
    switch nUserPage
      case 1
        l_bPage=true
      break
      case 2
        l_bPage=true
      break
      case 3
        l_bPage=true
      break
      case 4

      break
      default

      break
    endSwitch
    call uDate()
    delay(0)
  until (bExit or l_bPage)
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: HMI/uDate.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="uDate" access="private" >
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_sMonth" type="string" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  This subroutine reads the Date and Time of the Controller
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : June 2024 A. Cisneros (Stäubli)
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  switch (getDate("%m"))
    case "01"
      l_sMonth="January"
    break
    case "02"
      l_sMonth="February"
    break
    case "03"
      l_sMonth="March"
    break
    case "04"
      l_sMonth="April"
    break
    case "05"
      l_sMonth="May"
    break
    case "06"
      l_sMonth="June"
    break
    case "07"
      l_sMonth="July"
    break
    case "08"
      l_sMonth="August"
    break
    case "09"
      l_sMonth="September"
    break
    case "10"
      l_sMonth="October"
    break
    case "11"
      l_sMonth="November"
    break
    case "12"
      l_sMonth="December"
    break
    default
      l_sMonth="???"
    break
  endSwitch
  // Display date and date in the form: "January 01, 2007 13:45:23"
  sDate=getDate(l_sMonth+" %d, %Y %H:%M:%S")
end]]></Code>
  </Program>

===== FILE: HMI/uOptions.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="uOptions" access="private" >
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_bMagEdge" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bMagLast" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
      <Local name="l_bPage" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  This is the "Options" user interface.
  //      Displays:
  //              * Status of the robot (program pointer)
  //              * Temperatures of the Controller and Arm.
  //              * Status of Inputs and Outputs
  //              * (Sep 2026) the belt laser sensor lamps and counters
  //
  //  ***  THE MANUAL MAGNET BUTTON IS NOW EDGE-TRIGGERED AND CYCLE-GATED  ***
  //  (Sep 2026, Cowork/Claude - fixes "gripper will not pick while the
  //   Options page is displayed")
  //
  //  WHAT WAS WRONG. The block at the bottom of this loop used to read:
  //      if bMagnetManual==true and dioGet(dDOTool)==0
  //        call tGripperEMH("ON")
  //      elseIf bMagnetManual==false and dioGet(dDOTool)==1
  //        call tGripperEMH("OFF")
  //      endIf
  //  That is not a button handler. It is a SERVO: on every pass of this loop
  //  it forces the magnet to match bMagnetManual. And dDOTool is not a
  //  request - tkIOMonitor latches it from the coils themselves:
  //      if dioGet(dDOemh_gripperON)==1  -> dioSet(dDOTool,1)
  //      if dioGet(dDOemh_gripperOFF)==1 -> dioSet(dDOTool,0)
  //  So the servo was watching the magnet's ACTUAL state and driving it back
  //  to the button, which fights tkCycle on every single pick:
  //      1. tkCycle grips:  dioSet(dDOemh_gripperON,1), then delay(nEMH_onDelay)
  //      2. tkIOMonitor (delay(0), so within a tick): dDOTool = 1
  //      3. this loop (delay(0) too): bMagnetManual is false and dDOTool is 1,
  //         so it calls tGripperEMH("OFF") and pulses dDOemh_gripperOFF
  //  The release pulse therefore lands DURING the 0.5 s grip pulse - both
  //  coils energised at once - and the plate is demagnetised at the moment it
  //  was supposed to be picked. From the pendant it looks exactly like "the
  //  gripper will not cycle to pick a part", and it only happens on this page,
  //  because tkHMI only runs uOptions while nUserPage==2.
  //  The mirror image is just as bad: with bMagnetManual left TRUE, this loop
  //  re-magnetises immediately after Part_Accept's release, so the plate stays
  //  stuck to the tool or the next one is snatched on approach.
  //  This is ORIGINAL code (identical in every backup back to 2026-08-26) and
  //  nothing in the instrumentation or sensor work touched it. What changed is
  //  that there is now a reason to sit on the Options page during production -
  //  the sensor lamps and the heartbeat readout - so a latent fault that
  //  needed someone to be watching this page finally got someone watching it.
  //
  //  WHAT IT DOES NOW. Two changes, both in this file only:
  //    * EDGE, NOT LEVEL. The magnet is pulsed only when bMagnetManual
  //      actually CHANGES - i.e. only when the operator presses the button.
  //      l_bMagLast is seeded from bMagnetManual on entry, so merely opening
  //      the page never pulses anything. The magnet's own state is no longer
  //      consulted at all, so there is nothing left to fight over.
  //      A press that is ignored still updates l_bMagLast, so the button and
  //      the edge detector never drift apart: pressing it twice during a cycle
  //      leaves the magnet exactly as the cycle left it, which is the point.
  //    * CYCLE-GATED. taskStatus("cycle")==-1 is the same test monitoring()
  //      and killAllTask() use for "the cycle task is not running". A press
  //      while a cycle is live is IGNORED and logged, in BOTH directions: an
  //      ON mid-cycle would grab a plate on the approach, and an OFF mid-cycle
  //      would drop the one in the tool. Ignoring the press is the safe
  //      outcome. The button keeps working for what it is for - jogging the
  //      magnet at the pendant with the cell stopped.
  //  bStart is deliberately NOT the gate: it ships true in the .dtx, so it
  //  reads true on a fresh boot with the cell idle and would block the button
  //  when it should work. taskStatus is the real state.
  //
  //  NOT VERIFIED HERE. This cannot be compiled or run from where it was
  //  written. Check in the VAL3 IDE that l_bMagLast compiles as a bool local
  //  and that taskStatus("cycle") is accepted in this task, then test it with
  //  the cell STOPPED: the button should still make and break the magnet, once
  //  per press. Then test with a cycle running: the button should do nothing
  //  and put one line in the log.
  //
  //History
  //  Creation : June 2024 A. Cisneros (Stäubli)
  //  Sep 2026 : manual magnet made edge-triggered and gated on
  //             taskStatus("cycle"). No motion, no descriptor, no pick/place
  //             logic and no IO addressing changed - the only dio access this
  //             program ever made was the dioGet(dDOTool) that is now gone.
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  userPage("UserPageOptions")
  l_bPage=false
  //  Seed the edge detector from the button's CURRENT state, so that opening
  //  this page is never itself an edge. Only == and "and" are used below, on
  //  plain bool reads with no side effects, so nothing here depends on VAL3
  //  short-circuiting an "and" - which the reference manual does not promise.
  l_bMagLast=bMagnetManual
  l_bMagEdge=false
  do

    //--------------------
    //  User Interface
    //--------------------
    switch nUserPage
      case 1
        l_bPage=true
      break
      case 2

      break
      case 3
        l_bPage=true
      break
      case 4
        l_bPage=true
      break
      default

      break
    endSwitch
    call uDate()
    delay(0)

    //--------------------
    //  Manual magnet - operator button only, and only with the cell stopped
    //--------------------
    if bMagnetManual==true and l_bMagLast==false
      l_bMagLast=true
      l_bMagEdge=true
    elseIf bMagnetManual==false and l_bMagLast==true
      l_bMagLast=false
      l_bMagEdge=true
    endIf
    if l_bMagEdge==true
      l_bMagEdge=false
      if taskStatus("cycle")==-1
        if bMagnetManual==true
          call tGripperEMH("ON")
        else
          call tGripperEMH("OFF")
        endIf
      else
        logMsg("RAIV/T1: manual magnet button IGNORED - the cycle task is running. Stop the cycle before using it.",2)
      endIf
    endIf
  until (bExit or l_bPage)
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>

===== FILE: HMI/uProduction.pgx =====
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8" ?>
<Programs xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance" xmlns="http://www.staubli.com/robotics/VAL3/Program/2" >
  <Program name="uProduction" access="private" >
    <Locals>
      <Local name="l_bPage" type="bool" xsi:type="array" size="1" />
    </Locals>
    <Code><![CDATA[begin
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  //Header begin
  //
  //Description of program
  //  This is the "Production" user interface.
  //      Displays:
  //              * Status of the robot (program pointer)
  //              * Pipeline for APERA vision system
  //              * Program interface (allows to Start, Pause, EndCycle, Abort and move robot to Home position safely)
  //History
  //  Creation : June 2024 A. Cisneros (Stäubli)
  //Header end
  //-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  userPage("UserPageProduction")
  l_bPage=false
  do
    //--------------------
    //  Action Buttons
    //-------------------- 
    //DryCycle, CheckBox/Pipeline
    if bStart==false
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnEndCycle","disabled",true)
      //userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnDryCycle","disabled",false)
    else
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnEndCycle","disabled",false)
      //userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnDryCycle","disabled",true)
    endIf
    //
    //Start Button
    if bStart==false and sPipeline!=""
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnStart","style.backgroundColor","#B6B6B6")
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnStart","disabled",false)
    else
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnStart","disabled",true)
    endIf
    //
    //Pause/Resume Button
    if bPause==false
      restartMove()
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnPause","style.backgroundColor","#B6B6B6")
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnPause","innerHTML","Pause")
      if bStart==true and bPause==false
        userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnStart","style.backgroundColor","#47FF86")
      endIf
    else
      stopMove()
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnPause","style.backgroundColor","#FFC166")
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnPause","innerHTML","Resume")
      if (bStart==false and bPause==true) or (bStart==true and bPause==true)
        userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnStart","style.backgroundColor","#B6B6B6")
      endIf
    endIf
    //
    //Abort Button
    if bAbort==false
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnAbort","style.backgroundColor","#B6B6B6")
    endIf
    //
    //End Cycle
    if bEndCycleRobot==false
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnEndCycle","disabled",false)
    else
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnEndCycle","disabled",true)
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnEndCycle","style.backgroundColor","#9036FF")
    endIf
    //
    //--------------------
    //  DryCycle
    //--------------------  
    if bDryCycle
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnDryCycle","style.backgroundImage","pictures/switch_ON.png")
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","lblDryCycle","innerHTML","Dry Cycle ON")
    else
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","btnDryCycle","style.backgroundImage","pictures/switch_OFF.png")
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","lblDryCycle","innerHTML","Dry Cycle OFF")
    endIf
    //
    //--------------------
    //  OUTPUTS
    //--------------------    
    //Magnet ON
    if tAR70.gripper==true
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","recIMagnetON","style.backgroundColor","#404040 ")
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","lblMagnetOn","innerHTML","Magnet ON")
    else
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","recIMagnetON","style.backgroundColor","#D9D9D9")
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","lblMagnetOn","innerHTML","Magnet OFF")
    endIf
    //
    //Enable Valve 1
    if dDOValve1Enable==true
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","recIEnableValve1","style.backgroundColor","#404040 ")
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","lblEnableValve1","innerHTML","Enable Valve 1")
    else
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","recIEnableValve1","style.backgroundColor","#D9D9D9")
      userPageSet("UserPageProduction","lblEnableValve1","innerHTML","Disable Valve 1")
    endIf
    //

    //--------------------
    //  User Interface
    //--------------------     
    switch nUserPage
      case 1

      break
      case 2
        l_bPage=true
      break
      case 3
        l_bPage=true
      break
      case 4
        l_bPage=true
      break
      default
      break
    endSwitch
    call uDate()
    delay(0)
  until (bExit or l_bPage)
end]]></Code>
  </Program>
</Programs>
